226 of 226 questions

Question 1

Which one of the following is not correct when mapping ER diagrams to relational tables?

  • A. Multi-valued attributes are mapped to new tables
  • B. Foreign key are used to map relationships to create relation between tables
  • C. Table names are directly mapped from entity names
  • D. Composite attribute are mapped as it is

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 21

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply the ER mapping rule for the given attribute or entity type.

Study-guide sections

FDB10 · Map entities, composite and multivalued attributes

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB10 · Map entities, composite and multivalued attributes

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Correct answer: D — Composite attribute are mapped as it is

Atomic relational values exclude storing an undecomposed composite attribute. Relation names need not differ from entity names.

Evidence in the complete study note

Create one relation per strong entity type, preserve its simple attributes and select a candidate key. Expand composite attributes into their useful simple components; do not copy a composite ER structure unchanged into a flat atomic design. Derived values can usually be calculated, avoiding stale stored copies.FDB10
Table names may follow entity names; naming does not invalidate a mapping.FDB10
Map each multivalued attribute to a separate relation containing the owner key and one value.FDB10

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Multivalues are separated to remove repeating groups (PDF 12, 17.)

B · incorrect — Foreign-key mapping is explicitly described (PDF 12, 17.)

C · incorrect — Naming a relation after its entity is compatible with the mapping (PDF 12, 17.)

D · correct — Keeping a composite value unchanged violates atomicity (PDF 12, 17.)

Question 2

In DBMS-independent logical relational design, before vendor-specific refinement, which statement is NOT correct?

  • A. It consider a particular DBMS
  • B. Normalization is undertaken in logical phase
  • C. It is based on specific data model
  • D. It is independent from physical considerations

Adapted from

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 38

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the design phase from its inputs and intended deliverable.

Study-guide sections

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Correct answer: A — A. It consider a particular DBMS

Logical design maps the conceptual model into relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. It therefore uses a particular data model while separating storage decisions from logical structure. Under the stated DBMS-independent methodology, considering a particular vendor's DBMS is the false claim. Normalization, a chosen model and independence from physical storage are all consistent with this phase.

Evidence in the complete study note

First collect business facts, required transactions, reports, volumes, privacy rules and quality targets. Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model. Logical design maps the conceptual model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical design chooses file organization, indexes and storage. Implementation creates and loads the actual objects; testing checks requirements.FDB05
Whether logical design is DBMS-dependent depends on the stated design methodology.FDB05
Logical relational design is tied to a data model but can remain independent of a particular DBMS.FDB05

Choice by choice

A · correct — False in the stated phase: particular-DBMS implementation choices are deferred.

B · incorrect — True: normalization checks the mapped relations.

C · incorrect — True: relational logical design uses a specific data model.

D · incorrect — True: file layouts and indexes belong to physical design.

Question 3

Which SQL command would you use if you want to specify constraints to be applied on tuples?

  • A. GRANT
  • B. SELECT
  • C. REVOKE
  • D. CHECK

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 40

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read each SQL constraint's scope and enforcement rule.

Study-guide sections

FDB16 · Write and enforce SQL constraints

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB16 · Write and enforce SQL constraints

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: D — CHECK

CHECK verifies criteria on attribute values within rows; GRANT and REVOKE control permission, and SELECT retrieves data.

Evidence in the complete study note

Example: CREATE TABLE Enrollment (StudentID INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES Student(StudentID), CourseID INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES Course(CourseID), Grade INTEGER CHECK (Grade BETWEEN 0 AND 100), PRIMARY KEY (StudentID,CourseID)); Each column declaration gives its name then type, with commas between declarations. CHECK is a constraint clause, not a standalone GRANT-like statement. POSITIVE is not a general SQL constraint keyword; CHECK (Amount > 0) expresses positivity.FDB16
A CHECK can pass when its result is unknown, so add NOT NULL if missing values must be forbidden.FDB16
DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Permission grant is not a tuple predicate (PDF 49, 54.)

B · incorrect — Retrieval is not constraint specification (PDF 49, 54.)

C · incorrect — Permission removal is not a tuple predicate (PDF 49, 54.)

D · correct — CHECK is explicitly shown verifying criteria (PDF 49, 54.)

Question 4

Which data management approach allows application programs to define and manage their own data?

  • A. Manual approach
  • B. Database approach
  • C. Hierarchical approach
  • D. Traditional file based approach

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 41

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Review the database approach and match each claimed benefit or cost to its mechanism.

Study-guide sections

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Correct answer: D — Traditional file based approach

The file approach lacks the database approach separation between programs and data; application-owned data corresponds to traditional file processing. B reverses that distinction.

Evidence in the complete study note

A database is a coherent collection of related facts with meaning for an application. A DBMS is software that defines, stores, retrieves, updates, protects and shares that data. The database is the content; the DBMS manages it. A database system also includes applications, people and infrastructure.FDB01
Traditional file applications define their own formats and access procedures.FDB01
Data abstraction hides irrelevant details; program–data insulation separates data definitions from application code.FDB04

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Manual records do not describe application-owned files (PDF 5, 6.)

B · incorrect — Program/data insulation contradicts the stem (PDF 5, 6.)

C · incorrect — Hierarchical is a model rather than this processing distinction (PDF 5, 6.)

D · correct — Traditional file processing matches the contrast (PDF 5, 6.)

Question 5

Which database constraints are specified in the data definition language?

  • A. Implicit or inherent model based constraints
  • B. Semantic constraints
  • C. Application based constraints
  • D. Schema based constraints

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 43

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answer: D — Schema-based constraints.

The question asks how constraints are specified, rather than which business meaning they express. FDB15 distinguishes constraints inherent in a data model from constraints explicitly declared in schema DDL. That second category is schema-based. Semantic or application rules may require triggers or application code when ordinary DDL cannot express them.

Evidence in the complete study note

Inherent constraints come from the data model; schema-based constraints are declared through the DDL; semantic/application rules may need triggers or application logic when DDL cannot express them. Integrity improves data validity; authorization controls who may access it. Uniqueness and domain validation are both integrity mechanisms. If a Department primary key is NULL and Manager=107 lacks a referenced employee, entity integrity certainly fails and referential integrity also fails if that foreign key is declared. One INSERT can violate multiple constraints.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Inherent model constraints follow from the data model rather than explicit schema declarations.

B · incorrect — Semantic rules express application meaning and may exceed what ordinary schema DDL can enforce.

C · incorrect — Application-based constraints may require program logic rather than declarations in the database definition.

D · correct — Schema-based constraints are explicitly specified in data definition language declarations.

Question 6

Which SQL command removes an existing relation's table object, including its definition?

  • A. Delete
  • B. Drop
  • C. Remove
  • D. UPDATE

Adapted from

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 80

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — B. Drop

Removing a relation object means removing its definition as well as its contents. DROP TABLE performs that operation. DELETE affects rows while retaining the table, and UPDATE changes values within rows. REMOVE is not the SQL table-removal command. The distinction is between schema removal and operations on data in an existing schema.

Evidence in the complete study note

DELETE removes rows and keeps the table; DROP TABLE removes the table object; ALTER changes its definition. TRUNCATE empties a table using implementation-specific mechanisms. PostgreSQL allows transactional rollback of TRUNCATE, so no rollback information is retained is not a universal SQL property. SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific. SQLite's .databases lists attached names/files in its command-line shell; .database is not the documented command.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — DELETE removes rows and retains the table definition.

B · correct — DROP TABLE removes the table object and its definition.

C · incorrect — REMOVE is not the SQL command for dropping a table.

D · incorrect — UPDATE changes existing row values.

Question 7

Which of the following statement best describe relational data model?

  • A. It is a database model that allows multiple records to be linked to the same owner file.
  • B. It structures data in a tree like structure using parent to child relationship.
  • C. It is a database model to manage data as tuples grouped into relations
  • D. It is a model that use concepts such as entities, attributes, and relationships
  • E. Describe how data is stored in the computer, representing information such as record structures, record ordering, and access paths.

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 8

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare the data models by how they organize records and relationships.

Study-guide sections

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: C — It is a database model to manage data as tuples grouped into relations

A relational database consists of relations with tuple rows and attribute columns.

Evidence in the complete study note

A data model supplies concepts for structure, relationships, constraints and operations. Conceptual ER models describe entities and relationships. Relational models use tables; hierarchical models organize records in a parent–child tree; classic network models connect records through pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. Object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. A physical model specifies record layouts and access paths. These are different representation choices, not stages of one query.FDB02
Degree counts columns; cardinality counts rows.FDB03
A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property.FDB03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Owner-file links do not define tuple relations (PDF 13, 7.)

B · incorrect — A tree is not the stated relational representation (PDF 13, 7.)

C · correct — Tuple rows in relations match the definition (PDF 13, 7.)

D · incorrect — Entities and relationships describe conceptual ER modeling (PDF 13, 7.)

E · incorrect — Storage details define the physical model (PDF 13, 7.)

Question 8

Department(Dept_Name, Manager) has primary key Dept_Name and foreign key Manager referencing Employee(Emp_ID). Employee contains IDs 101–105 only. Consider INSERT INTO Department (Dept_Name, Manager) VALUES (NULL, 107); Which named integrity rules are violated? Select all that apply. Treat key integrity as uniqueness and domain integrity as type/allowed-value checks.

  • A. Domain constraint
  • B. Key constraint
  • C. Entity Integrity constraint
  • D. Referential Integrity constraint

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 9

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answers (select all): C, D — C. Entity Integrity constraint; D. Referential Integrity constraint

The inserted row has two independent defects. Dept_Name is the primary key, so NULL violates entity integrity. Manager is a non-NULL foreign key, yet employee 107 is absent, violating referential integrity. These are separate requirements and the same insertion fails both. Under the question's taxonomy, neither a duplicate-key violation nor an incompatible-domain value is being asserted.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The stated domain checks are satisfied; primary-key NULL is classified separately.

B · incorrect — No duplicate key value is inserted; this category tests uniqueness.

C · correct — NULL in the primary key violates entity integrity.

D · correct — Manager 107 has no referenced employee and violates referential integrity.

Question 9

Assume atomic attribute values. Consider R(A,B,C,D) with functional dependencies F = {C→D, C→B, B→C}. Which statement about R is true?

  • A. The relation R is in second normal form
  • B. The relation R is in third normal form
  • C. The candidate key for R is BC
  • D. The relation R is in Boyce–Codd normal form
  • E. None of the other statements is true

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 10

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Derive every candidate key before testing dependency-based normal forms.

Study-guide sections

FDB18 · Find all keys before deciding normal form

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB18 · Find all keys before deciding normal form

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: E — None of the other statements is true

Every candidate key must contain A because no dependency derives it. Both AB and AC determine all attributes and are minimal; BC determines only B, C and D. The nonprime attribute D depends on B or C alone, a proper subset of either composite key, so 2NF fails. The dependency C→D also violates 3NF and BCNF because C is not a superkey and D is nonprime. None of the other statements is true.

Evidence in the complete study note

For R(A,B,C,D), let C→D, C→B and B→C. A never appears on a right-hand side, so every key needs A. (AB)+ gives ABCD through B→C→D; (AC)+ also gives ABCD. AB and AC are minimal keys. BC cannot derive A and is not a key. A, B and C are prime; D is nonprime.FDB18
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20
For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — False: D depends on a proper subset of a candidate key, violating 2NF.

B · incorrect — False: C→D has a nonsuperkey determinant and a nonprime dependent attribute, violating 3NF.

C · incorrect — False: (BC)+ = {B,C,D}, so BC cannot determine A and is not a key.

D · incorrect — False: C determines D but is not a superkey, violating BCNF.

E · correct — Correct: each of the other four claims is false.

Question 10

Which schema of the three-schema Architecture describe the structure of the whole database?

  • A. Internal schema
  • B. Conceptual schema
  • C. External schema
  • D. schema

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 11

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace which schema levels a proposed change affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Correct answer: B — Conceptual schema

The conceptual schema describes the whole database while internal describes storage and external describes each user view.

Evidence in the complete study note

The external level gives each user group a selected view. The conceptual level describes the whole logical database: entity types, relationships, data types and constraints. The internal/physical level describes files, record placement and access paths. These are abstraction levels, distinct from client/server deployment tiers.FDB04

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Internal covers physical storage (PDF 8.)

B · correct — Conceptual explicitly covers the whole database (PDF 8.)

C · incorrect — External covers a user subset (PDF 8.)

D · incorrect — Generic schema does not identify the required level (PDF 8.)

Question 11

Which of the following is the disadvantage of a database approach?

  • A. Data consistency
  • B. Sharing of data
  • C. Improved data integrity
  • D. Increased concurrency
  • E. Enforcement of standards
  • F. Complexity
  • G. None

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 12

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Review the database approach and match each claimed benefit or cost to its mechanism.

Study-guide sections

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

Correct answer: F — Complexity is a database-approach disadvantage.

FDB01 identifies complexity and administration costs as possible disadvantages of a DBMS. Consistency, sharing, integrity and centrally supported standards are benefits. Increased concurrency means useful concurrent access here; only uncontrolled concurrent access causes anomalies. Thus complexity is the sole listed disadvantage, even though database management can improve the other listed properties.

Evidence in the complete study note

A database is a coherent collection of related facts with meaning for an application. A DBMS is software that defines, stores, retrieves, updates, protects and shares that data. The database is the content; the DBMS manages it. A database system also includes applications, people and infrastructure.FDB01
Concurrent access itself is useful; uncontrolled concurrent access causes anomalies.FDB01
Separate files can repeat facts, disagree after updates, isolate useful data and make shared integrity or concurrent updates difficult.FDB01

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Data consistency is improved by shared definitions and controlled updates, making it a benefit.

B · incorrect — Sharing data is a purpose and benefit of the database approach.

C · incorrect — Improved integrity means better enforcement of valid data and is a benefit.

D · incorrect — Concurrent access is useful; uncontrolled interference, rather than increased concurrency itself, causes problems.

E · incorrect — Central management supports consistent definitions and standards, which is a benefit.

F · correct — A DBMS introduces administrative and technical complexity that can outweigh benefits for small applications.

G · incorrect — A listed disadvantage exists: the complexity of database management.

Question 12

Which of the following is a correct statement about relation? (Choose all that applies)

  • A. Values of each columns is a repeating group/array
  • B. Values in a column can be from different domain
  • C. A relation is in the INF
  • D. Two rows in a relational table can be identical
  • E. The sequence of columns and rows is insignificant
  • F. None

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 13

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

Study-guide sections

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

Correct answers (select all): C, E — A relation is in the INF; The sequence of columns and rows is insignificant

Read INF as 1NF. Relations have atomic cells rather than repeating groups; the relational representation is independent of tuple sequence. The column sequence likewise does not alter named attributes. Distinct tuples and typed attribute domains rule out A,B,D.

Evidence in the complete study note

A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table. A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property. Degree counts columns; cardinality counts rows. Classical relations have distinct tuples, atomic cells and domains for attributes; row order has no meaning. Column order has no logical meaning when attributes are identified by name. SQL tables can contain duplicate rows unless constrained.FDB03
1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells.FDB20
A mapped ER design that correctly splits composite and multivalued structures gives ordinary 1NF tables; ER drawing alone guarantees no higher form.FDB20

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Repeating arrays violate atomicity (PDF 14, 17.)

B · incorrect — An attribute has a constrained domain (PDF 14, 17.)

C · correct — Atomic relation values satisfy 1NF (PDF 14, 17.)

D · incorrect — Duplicate tuples conflict with relation uniqueness (PDF 14, 17.)

E · correct — Presentation order does not change named tuple values (PDF 14, 17.)

F · incorrect — C and E already supply valid statements (PDF 14, 17.)

Question 13

Which of the following statement is correct about executing the following SQL query on the employee database given in question 2? (Choose all that applies) SELECT Emp_ID, First_Name, Last_Name FROM Employee E, Department D WHERE Department <> Sales' and AVG (salary) > 35000 and E.Department=D.Dept_Name GROUP BY Department;

  • A. the query will execute successfully and will obtain all departments with an average salary higher than 35,000
  • B. the query will not execute because Department attribute in Group by statement was not included in the select statement list
  • C. the query will not execute because we can't include aggregate function in the where statement
  • D. the query will execute successfully and will obtain employees of all departments with an average salary higher than 35,000

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 14

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply grouping before filtering groups and computing aggregate output.

Study-guide sections

FDB32 · Aggregation, GROUP BY and HAVING

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB32 · Aggregation, GROUP BY and HAVING

Correct answer: C — AVG(salary) cannot be used directly in WHERE.

The query places AVG(salary) > 35000 in WHERE. FDB32 requires ordinary aggregate predicates in HAVING. Omitting Department from SELECT is not itself an error. Selecting ungrouped employee details is another defect unless functional dependence is established. The query therefore does not successfully produce either claimed result.

Evidence in the complete study note

COUNT(*) counts rows, COUNT(x) counts non-NULL x values; SUM and AVG summarize non-NULL numeric values. SQL's usual average function is AVG, not AVERAGE. GROUP BY creates one group per distinct grouping value; HAVING tests aggregate groups after WHERE has filtered individual rows.FDB32
The grouping column need not itself appear in SELECT; check the functional dependence of other selected columns.FDB32
SELECT Department, AVG(Salary) FROM Employee WHERE Department <> 'Sales' GROUP BY Department HAVING AVG(Salary) > 35000; lists qualifying department averages.FDB32

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The aggregate predicate in WHERE prevents this query from successfully producing department averages.

B · incorrect — A grouping column need not appear in SELECT; that omission is not the stated error.

C · correct — WHERE filters individual rows before grouping, so the ordinary AVG predicate belongs in HAVING.

D · incorrect — The invalid aggregate predicate prevents execution; employee columns also require valid grouping or functional dependence.

Question 14

Which of the following keys is generally used to represent the relationships between the tables?

  • A. None of the choices is correct
  • B. Secondary key
  • C. Primary key
  • D. Foreign key

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 21

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Correct answer: D — Foreign key

A foreign key is the child-side reference linking to an existing parent candidate key.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15
A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys.FDB07
Secondary indexes provide other access paths and must represent all searchable values with record pointers or pointer lists.FDB25

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A named key does supply the link (PDF 48.)

B · incorrect — Secondary key is not the child reference (PDF 48.)

C · incorrect — Primary key is the target identifier (PDF 48.)

D · correct — Foreign key is the explicit link (PDF 48.)

Question 16

Which one of the following given statements possibly contains the error?

  • A. select empid where empid = 1009 and Lastname = 'ADDIS';
  • B. select empid from emp where empid = 10006;
  • C. select empid from emp;
  • D. select * from emp where empid = 10003;

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace how SELECT forms its result columns and rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB27 · SELECT and its result columns

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB27 · SELECT and its result columns

Correct answer: A — The first SELECT is missing its FROM source.

The first query reads empid and Lastname but never names a table in FROM. FDB27 allows literal-only SELECT statements without FROM, but this statement is not literal-only. The other three statements include emp as their source and use ordinary valid selection syntax, assuming those table and column names exist.

Evidence in the complete study note

SELECT Name,Address FROM Customer; produces only those mailing-label fields. SELECT * selects all columns; DISTINCT removes duplicate result rows; AS supplies an alias; ALL permits duplicates and does not mean all columns. SQL normally preserves duplicates without DISTINCT. A query reading table columns needs an appropriate FROM source. A literal-only query such as SELECT 1 may legally omit FROM.FDB27
SELECT alone normally reads without changing the database state.FDB27
Typical written clause order is SELECT … FROM … WHERE … GROUP BY … HAVING … ORDER BY ….FDB27

Choice by choice

A · correct — It refers to table columns without a FROM clause identifying their source.

B · incorrect — It selects empid from emp with a syntactically ordinary equality predicate.

C · incorrect — It supplies emp as the table source and validly selects its empid column.

D · incorrect — It supplies a table source, selects all columns, and filters by empid.

Question 17

Which one of the following is a type of Data Manipulation Command?

  • A. Delete
  • B. Alter
  • C. All choices are correct
  • D. Create

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 24

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: A — Delete

DELETE is explicitly DML; ALTER and CREATE are DDL.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · correct — DELETE is in DML list (PDF 26, 26.)

B · incorrect — ALTER is in DDL list (PDF 26, 26.)

C · incorrect — Other choices include DDL (PDF 26, 26.)

D · incorrect — CREATE is in DDL list (PDF 26, 26.)

Question 18

Which one of the following refers to the "data about data"?

  • A. Warehouse
  • B. Directory
  • C. Sub Data
  • D. Meta Data

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 25

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

Study-guide sections

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: D — Meta Data

The description of database structure and constraints is metadata.

Evidence in the complete study note

A schema describes names, structures and constraints; an instance, state or snapshot is the data at a particular time. Inserting a student changes the state, while adding a column changes the schema. Metadata is data describing data. The catalog or data dictionary records table definitions, types, constraints and often access privileges and optimizer statistics. A schema diagram draws tables and key references; labels such as database diagram can overlap.FDB03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Warehouse is not this term (PDF 5.)

B · incorrect — Directory is not this term (PDF 5.)

C · incorrect — Sub Data is not this term (PDF 5.)

D · correct — Meta-data describes the data structure (PDF 5.)

Question 19

A language that used to formulate the schema of database structure is______.

  • A. Data Manipulation language
  • B. Data Definition Language
  • C. Transaction Control Language
  • D. Data Control Language

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 29

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 29

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — Data Definition Language

DDL supplies definitions, while DML handles data operations.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Question 20

A table can have only one

  • A. Unique Key
  • B. Candidate key
  • C. Primary key
  • D. Foreign key

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 30

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 30

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Test uniqueness and minimality separately when classifying keys.

Study-guide sections

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answer: C — Primary key

One candidate is selected as the primary key; multiple candidates and multiple inter-table references can coexist.

Evidence in the complete study note

A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple. A candidate key is a minimal superkey: removing any component loses uniqueness. Minimal means no unnecessary attribute, not necessarily the fewest columns among all keys. A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys. A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07
A table can have several candidates and foreign keys but at most one declared primary-key constraint, possibly containing several columns.FDB07
Prefer stable, compact identifiers when practical, but Email, SSN or EmployeeID is a candidate only if business rules guarantee uniqueness.FDB07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Uniqueness need not be the selected identifier (PDF 11, 11.)

B · incorrect — Several candidates are explicitly allowed (PDF 11, 11.)

C · correct — Primary key is the selected candidate (PDF 11, 11.)

D · incorrect — Separate relationships can use separate foreign keys (PDF 11, 11.)

Question 21

When the values in one or more attributes being used as a foreign key must exist there must be a corresponding primary key value in another table in the database, we have created

  • A. Domain Integrity constraint
  • B. Referential Integrity Constraint
  • C. Transitive Dependency
  • D. Entity Integrity Constraints

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 32

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 32

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

FDB17 · Functional dependencies and attribute closure

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

FDB17 · Functional dependencies and attribute closure

Correct answer: B — Referential Integrity Constraint

Matching foreign-key values to existing parent occurrences is the referential-integrity rule.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15
Full dependency requires all determinant attributes; partial dependency holds through a proper subset.FDB17
A prime attribute belongs to some candidate key; a nonprime attribute belongs to none.FDB17

B · correct — Matches the explicit parent-reference rule (PDF 48.)

C · incorrect — Transitivity is functional dependency (PDF 48.)

D · incorrect — Entity integrity constrains the primary key itself (PDF 48.)

Question 22

Which of the following is not a feature of DBMS?

  • A. Support ACID Property
  • B. Single-user Access only
  • C. High Level of Security
  • D. Minimum Duplication and Redundancy of Data

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

FDB37 · Transactions and the ACID properties

Correct answer: B — Single-user Access only

Single-user-only contradicts the explicit multiuser DBMS requirement.

Evidence in the complete study note

A DBMS centralizes metadata and supports multiple views, authorization, transactions, backup and recovery. It controls redundancy rather than guaranteeing zero duplication.FDB01
A transaction groups operations into one logical unit. Atomicity applies all its effects or none. Consistency means a correct transaction preserves declared invariants. Isolation controls interference between concurrent transactions; serializable isolation provides behavior equivalent to some serial execution. Durability preserves committed effects after a crash, typically through durable logging rather than forcing every data page immediately.FDB37
Concurrent access itself is useful; uncontrolled concurrent access causes anomalies.FDB01

Question 23

Which of the following is not a function of the database?

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

Correct answer: A — Analyzing code

Data definition, manipulation and recovery are database responsibilities. Arbitrary program-code analysis is not a listed database function; parsing database queries is a narrower task.

Evidence in the complete study note

A database is a coherent collection of related facts with meaning for an application. A DBMS is software that defines, stores, retrieves, updates, protects and shares that data. The database is the content; the DBMS manages it. A database system also includes applications, people and infrastructure.FDB01
A DBMS centralizes metadata and supports multiple views, authorization, transactions, backup and recovery.FDB01

Question 24

Read each pair left→right. Buildings and locations are 1:1; each employee belongs to one department and a department may have many employees; a hospital serves many patients, each registered at one hospital; authors and books are M:N. Which pair is many-to-one?

  • A. [ Building — Location]
  • B. [ Employee — Department]
  • C. [ Hospital — Patient]
  • D. [ Author — Book]

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 38

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 38

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read the relationship in both directions and check participation constraints.

Study-guide sections

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Correct answer: B — B. [ Employee — Department]

Many-to-one means several left-side entities may share a right-side entity while each left entity has at most one right partner. Employees share departments under the stated assignment rule. Building→Location is one-to-one, Hospital→Patient is one-to-many and Author→Book is many-to-many. Reading the direction and both maximum counts determines the answer rather than the entity names.

Evidence in the complete study note

Cardinality specifies maximum associated occurrences: 1:1, 1:N or M:N. Minimum cardinality specifies optional/partial participation (zero permitted) or mandatory/total participation (at least one). A maximum of one does not mean participation is compulsory. Relationship degree counts participating entity roles: unary recursive, binary or ternary.FDB09
If each employee belongs to one department and each department has many employees, Employee→Department is N:1; Department→Employee is 1:N.FDB09
Building→Location could also be N:1, so names alone cannot select a unique example.FDB09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Building→Location is explicitly 1:1.

B · correct — Many employees can share one department; each employee has one department.

C · incorrect — Hospital→Patient is 1:N under the registration rule.

D · incorrect — Author→Book is explicitly M:N.

Question 25

__________ is a single elementary unit of information representing an attribute of an entity.

  • A. Entity Set
  • B. Record
  • C. File
  • D. Field

Source

  • Data Structures & Algorithms practice questions · Question 4

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

Study-guide sections

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

Correct answer: D — Field.

A field corresponds to one attribute rather than the collection of attributes describing a whole record. FDB03 equates attribute, column and field, while tuple, row and record refer to related attribute values together. An entity set groups entities, and a file groups stored records, so both are larger collections.

Evidence in the complete study note

A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table. A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property. Degree counts columns; cardinality counts rows. Classical relations have distinct tuples, atomic cells and domains for attributes; row order has no meaning. Column order has no logical meaning when attributes are identified by name. SQL tables can contain duplicate rows unless constrained.FDB03
An entity type describes common properties; its current collection of entities is an entity set.FDB06
In Chen notation rectangles represent entity types, diamonds relationships and ellipses attributes; double ellipses denote multivalued attributes and dashed ellipses derived attributes.FDB06

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — An entity set collects entities of one type rather than representing a single attribute.

B · incorrect — A record contains related values for multiple attributes, rather than one elementary field.

C · incorrect — A file groups records and is not an elementary attribute unit.

D · correct — A field represents one attribute or property within a record.

Question 26

An ER model has entity types A and B connected by an attribute-free relationship R. Cardinalities are read A→B. Which conditions allow R to be represented by a single B-key foreign-key column in A, permitting NULL for partial participation? Select all that apply.

  • A. Relationship R is one -to —many and the participation of A in R is Total.
  • B. Relationship R is one -to —many and the participation of A in R is Partial.
  • C. Relationship R is many —to —one and the participation of A in R is Total.
  • D. Relationship R is many —-to —one and the participation of A in R is Partial.

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam · 2015 · Question 17

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Locate the relationship's cardinality and participation before placing a foreign key.

Study-guide sections

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Correct answers (select all): C, D — C. Relationship R is many —to —one and the participation of A in R is Total.; D. Relationship R is many —-to —one and the participation of A in R is Partial.

A single foreign-key column in A can represent R whenever each A is related to at most one B. This is exactly the many-to-one direction. Total participation makes the reference mandatory; partial participation permits NULL. One-to-many in the opposite direction needs the reference on B or another representation, because one A may have multiple related B values.

Evidence in the complete study note

For 1:N, place the one-side key as a foreign key in the many-side table; store relationship attributes there. Department(DeptID) and Employee(EmpID,DeptID,HireDate) implement many employees per department. If participation is total, make the reference NOT NULL. If partial, a nullable reference can represent no assignment.FDB11
For A→B many-to-one, A can absorb the relationship reference under either total or partial participation; requiring total participation as the only possible case overstates the rule.FDB11
Prefer the totally participating side to reduce NULLs; NOT NULL enforces that side's mandatory reference.FDB11

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — For A→B one-to-many, B normally holds A's key; total participation does not reverse that mapping.

B · incorrect — Partial participation does not make a one-to-many collection fit a single ordinary foreign key in A.

C · correct — Many-to-one with total A participation stores a non-NULL B reference in A.

D · correct — Many-to-one with partial A participation stores a nullable B reference in A.

Question 27

Under set relational algebra, which listed operation is not commutative? Interpret Join as an inner join, adjust its predicate when swapping inputs, and ignore attribute order.

  • A. Union
  • B. Join
  • C. Intersection
  • D. None

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam · 2015 · Question 36

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply each algebra operator to the input relations step by step.

Study-guide sections

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: D — D. None

Union and intersection give the same tuple sets when their inputs are exchanged. An inner join also gives corresponding matches after the predicate is adjusted for the swapped inputs; the question explicitly ignores attribute order. Thus none of the three named operations lacks commutativity here. This conclusion depends on the stated inner-join interpretation; preservation of one designated outer side would use a different rule.

Evidence in the complete study note

Selection σ filters tuples; projection π retains attributes and removes duplicate tuples under set semantics. Rename ρ changes relation or attribute names. Union, difference and intersection require union-compatible schemas. Difference R−S is not commutative; union and intersection are. Inner joins are commutative up to attribute order when predicates are adjusted; left outer joins are not generally commutative. Identify the join variant before predicting its behavior.FDB35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Union is commutative on union-compatible relations.

B · incorrect — Inner join is commutative under the stated predicate and schema convention.

C · incorrect — Intersection is commutative on union-compatible relations.

D · correct — All three named operators are commutative under these assumptions.

Question 28

Which of the following is a benefit of normalization rather than a potential cost or disadvantage?

  • A. May require joins to reconstruct decomposed records
  • B. May reduce system performance
  • C. Time consuming
  • D. Reduce duplicate data

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam · 2015 · Question 64

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.

Study-guide sections

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: D — Reduce duplicate data

Normalization organizes facts according to their dependencies to reduce avoidable duplication and update anomalies. That reduction is a benefit. Decomposing a table may require joins when an application needs the original combined information, which can increase retrieval cost for some workloads. Dependency analysis and schema design also take time. These are possible costs, not claims that normalization always slows a system. Declared dependencies suffice for normalization even when the current sample data shows no errors.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normalization separates facts according to their determinants, reducing avoidable repetition and associated anomalies. It does not require seeing bad current data: declared dependencies suffice. Adding a generated row ID may make insertion technically possible, but does not remove repeated facts or fix the dependency design. Reduced redundancy can improve integrity and storage use, but extra keys and joins mean neither storage savings nor faster retrieval is guaranteed.FDB19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A possible cost: reconstructing information from decomposed tables may require joins.

B · incorrect — A possible cost: additional joins can reduce performance for some workloads, although this is not inevitable.

C · incorrect — A possible cost: analyzing dependencies and designing a decomposition takes time.

D · correct — Correct: reducing duplicate facts is a principal benefit, rather than a disadvantage.

Question 29

Let you have two relations A and B, each table has 200 and 100 rows of records respectively. So after applying cross product on those tables, how many rows of records you will have?

  • A. 300
  • B. 20000
  • C. 2000
  • D. 100

Source

  • Computer Science model exam · 2015 · Question 86

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply each algebra operator to the input relations step by step.

Study-guide sections

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: B — 20000

The Cartesian-product example explicitly creates 1000*50 tuples from inputs of sizes 1000 and 50 (PDF p.28, lines 6–8). Applying the same all-pairs construction to 200 and 100 rows yields 200*100 = 20000. Each of the 200 rows is paired with each of the 100 rows; no filtering or duplicate-eliminating projection is specified.

Evidence in the complete study note

A Cartesian product has |R|×|S| tuples and degree degree(R)+degree(S), renaming conflicting attributes if necessary. Thus 200×100=20,000 and 5×7=35. Join equals selection over a product; intersection can be derived from difference. A common minimal basis contains selection, projection, union, difference, product and rename. Assignment is a useful notation, not an indispensable primitive; lists of basic operations vary across textbooks.FDB35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — 300 adds input sizes instead of counting pairs (PDF p.28).

B · correct — 20000 is 200*100, the illustrated Cartesian pair-count rule (PDF p.28).

C · incorrect — 2000 is ten times too small for the all-pairs count (PDF p.28).

D · incorrect — 100 counts only one input and omits its 200 pairings per row (PDF p.28).

Question 31

Which database-design stage primarily builds an ER model from the users' requirements?

  • A. Applications and security
  • B. Physical refinement
  • C. Conceptual database
  • D. Schema refinement

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 17

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the design phase from its inputs and intended deliverable.

Study-guide sections

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Correct answer: C — Conceptual database

Conceptual design represents the required entities, attributes and relationships in an ER model. Logical design later maps that conceptual model into relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical refinement chooses storage organizations and access paths, while application and security work concerns behavior and protection rather than the modeling stage requested. The stem asks for the primary stage building the ER model, not every later activity that might consult it.

Evidence in the complete study note

First collect business facts, required transactions, reports, volumes, privacy rules and quality targets. Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model. Logical design maps the conceptual model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical design chooses file organization, indexes and storage. Implementation creates and loads the actual objects; testing checks requirements.FDB05

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: application and security work addresses behavior and protection, rather than building the conceptual ER model.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: physical refinement chooses storage organization and access paths.

C · correct — Correct: conceptual design turns requirements into the ER model.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: schema refinement checks or improves a logical schema, rather than naming the initial ER-model stage.

Question 32

Employee(empid, ...) already exists, has no primary key, and empid values are unique and non-NULL. Which SQL statement adds a primary key on empid?

  • A. ADD TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (empid)
  • B. ATTACH TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (empid)
  • C. ADD TABLE Employee ATTACH PRIMARY KEY (empid)
  • D. ALTER TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (empid);

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 19

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish table definition changes from changes to stored rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Correct answer: D — D. ALTER TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (empid);

Adding a constraint to an existing table uses ALTER TABLE, followed by ADD PRIMARY KEY and the parenthesized key column. The existing unique, non-NULL empid values satisfy the prerequisite for the constraint. Option D has this structure. ADD TABLE and ATTACH TABLE do not perform this alteration, and ATTACH PRIMARY KEY is not the required constraint clause.

Evidence in the complete study note

CREATE TABLE Employee (EmpID INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, Name VARCHAR(80)); declares two columns. The name and its type stay together; the comma separates whole column declarations. To add a primary key later: ALTER TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (EmpID); existing data must be unique and non-NULL. A table cannot gain a second primary key without replacing the existing constraint.FDB30

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — ADD TABLE does not modify an existing table's constraints.

B · incorrect — ATTACH TABLE is not the required table-alteration syntax.

C · incorrect — Neither ADD TABLE nor ATTACH PRIMARY KEY forms this SQL alteration.

D · correct — The corrected ALTER TABLE statement adds the primary-key constraint.

Question 33

While checking normalization process, the problem that doesn't exist on transitive dependency is

  • A. Modification Anomaly
  • B. Revoke Anomaly
  • C. Insertion Anomaly
  • D. Deletion Anomaly

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 20

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.

Study-guide sections

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: B — Revoke Anomaly

The defined anomalies are insertion, deletion and update; revoke concerns privileges.

Evidence in the complete study note

Suppose Enrollment repeats StudentName for every course. An update anomaly requires changing every copy of the student's name; missed copies disagree. An insertion anomaly prevents recording a new student until some enrollment exists. A deletion anomaly loses the only record of a student's details when their last enrollment is deleted. Revoke anomaly is not a normalization anomaly.FDB19
Normalization separates facts according to their determinants, reducing avoidable repetition and associated anomalies.FDB19
Adding a generated row ID may make insertion technically possible, but does not remove repeated facts or fix the dependency design.FDB19

B · correct — Revoke is not a normalization anomaly (PDF 16.)

Question 34

You need to produce a report for mailing labels for customers. The mailing label must have only the customer name and address. The CUSTOMERS table has these columns, ID ,NAME,ADDRESS,PHONE ,the SELECT statement to accomplishes this task? *

  • A. SELECT name, address FROM customers;
  • B. SELECT*FROM customers;
  • C. SELECT id, name, address, phone FROM customers;
  • D. SELECT cust_name, cust_address FROM customers;

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 21

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace how SELECT forms its result columns and rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB27 · SELECT and its result columns

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB27 · SELECT and its result columns

Correct answer: A — SELECT name, address FROM customers;

The required output contains exactly NAME and ADDRESS from the listed schema. FDB27 distinguishes naming specific columns from selecting all columns with *. A names the two available columns explicitly. B and C include unwanted ID and PHONE fields, while D uses column names not supplied by the question.

Evidence in the complete study note

SELECT Name,Address FROM Customer; produces only those mailing-label fields. SELECT * selects all columns; DISTINCT removes duplicate result rows; AS supplies an alias; ALL permits duplicates and does not mean all columns. SQL normally preserves duplicates without DISTINCT. A query reading table columns needs an appropriate FROM source. A literal-only query such as SELECT 1 may legally omit FROM.FDB27
Typical written clause order is SELECT … FROM … WHERE … GROUP BY … HAVING … ORDER BY ….FDB27
SELECT alone normally reads without changing the database state.FDB27

Choice by choice

A · correct — It selects exactly the two existing columns required for the mailing label.

B · incorrect — The wildcard also includes ID and PHONE, exceeding the required output.

C · incorrect — It explicitly includes ID and PHONE in addition to the requested label fields.

D · incorrect — The supplied schema names NAME and ADDRESS, not CUST_NAME and CUST_ADDRESS.

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

At most one related entity on each side is the one-to-one case.

Evidence in the complete study note

Cardinality specifies maximum associated occurrences: 1:1, 1:N or M:N. Minimum cardinality specifies optional/partial participation (zero permitted) or mandatory/total participation (at least one). A maximum of one does not mean participation is compulsory. Relationship degree counts participating entity roles: unary recursive, binary or ternary.FDB09

Question 36

Which SQL statement sets PRICE to 600 in BOOK for rows whose ID is 'B001'?

  • A. CHANGE BOOK SET PRICE =600 Where ID ='B001';
  • B. UPDATE TO CHANGE BOOK SET PRICE =600 Where ID ='B001';
  • C. UPDATE BOOK SET PRICE =600 Where ID ='B001';
  • D. GRANT BOOK SET PRICE =600 Where ID ='B001';

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track which rows each data-changing command affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: C — UPDATE BOOK SET PRICE =600 Where ID ='B001';

UPDATE changes existing row values. The table name follows UPDATE, SET supplies the assignment and WHERE restricts the rows. C therefore assigns 600 only where ID equals the quoted string B001. CHANGE is not the row-update command, the extra TO CHANGE words break the required UPDATE structure, and GRANT controls privileges rather than performing a row assignment. These malformed alternatives are definite incorrect selections.

Evidence in the complete study note

UPDATE Book SET Price = 600 WHERE ID = 'B001'; changes matching rows. UPDATE without WHERE targets every row. DELETE FROM Publisher WHERE Name = 'XWZ'; deletes matching publisher rows, subject to reference policies. DELETE FROM r; removes every row but leaves r defined. Both clear relation entries and delete rows describe that effect. Several UPDATE statements can all be valid while performing different tasks; most appropriate requires a requested change and a specified quoting dialect.FDB29
DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: the required row-change statement begins UPDATE, not CHANGE BOOK SET.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: UPDATE must be followed by its target table, not TO CHANGE.

C · correct — Correct: the statement assigns 600 to PRICE for precisely the matching ID rows.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: GRANT manages privileges; it cannot perform this SET assignment.

Question 37

Question 39

Removal of transitivity property should be full filled in

  • A. First Normal form
  • B. Fourth Normal form
  • C. Third Normal form
  • D. Second Normal form

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 26

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: C — Third Normal form

3NF requires nonprime attributes to depend nontransitively on keys after reaching 2NF.

Evidence in the complete study note

2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key. For Enrollment(SID,CID,SName,CTitle,Grade), assume key (SID,CID), SID→SName and CID→CTitle. Split into Student(SID,SName), Course(CID,CTitle) and Enrollment(SID,CID,Grade). Grade still depends on the whole pair. A relation whose every candidate key is single-attribute has no nonprime partial-key dependency. Higher forms inherit 2NF, so no partial dependencies establishes the minimum guaranteed stage only when 1NF is also assumed.FDB20
With atomic values the highest form is 1NF, even though a transitive dependency also exists.FDB21
The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — 1NF requires atomicity (PDF 18.)

B · incorrect — 4NF is not the stated rule (PDF 18.)

C · correct — 3NF explicitly removes transitive dependence (PDF 18.)

D · incorrect — 2NF removes partial dependence (PDF 18.)

Question 40

If relation R has 5 tuples and relation S has also 7 tuples, then Cartesian product of R and S will have

  • A. 5+7 tuples
  • B. 7-5 tuples
  • C. 5X7 tuples
  • D. 5/7 tuples

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 27

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply each algebra operator to the input relations step by step.

Study-guide sections

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: C — 5X7 tuples

The module explicitly multiplies input tuple counts for a Cartesian product (PDF p.28, lines 6–8). Hence 5 rows paired with 7 rows give 5*7 = 35 distinct concatenated tuples, before any filtering/projection.

Evidence in the complete study note

A Cartesian product has |R|×|S| tuples and degree degree(R)+degree(S), renaming conflicting attributes if necessary. Thus 200×100=20,000 and 5×7=35. Join equals selection over a product; intersection can be derived from difference. A common minimal basis contains selection, projection, union, difference, product and rename. Assignment is a useful notation, not an indispensable primitive; lists of basic operations vary across textbooks.FDB35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — 5+7 is the sum, not all pair combinations (PDF p.28).

B · incorrect — 7-5 is the size difference, not the pair count (PDF p.28).

C · correct — 5*7 gives the 35 combinations required by the product construction (PDF p.28).

D · incorrect — 5/7 is not even an integer tuple count and is not pair enumeration (PDF p.28).

Question 41

The language associated with a database management system that is employed by end users and programmers to alter data values in the database is the:

  • A. Data translation language.
  • B. Data definition language.
  • C. Data manipulation language.

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: C — Data manipulation language.

The language for INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE is DML.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Question 42

Using standard SQL string literals, which update changes only the name of the student whose ID is '123' to 'abel'?

  • A. UPDATE student ID='123';
  • B. UPDATE student SET ID='123';
  • C. UPDATE student SET name='abel' WHERE ID='123';
  • D. UPDATE student SET name='abel';

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 71

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track which rows each data-changing command affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

FDB28 · Predicates, strings and NULL

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

FDB28 · Predicates, strings and NULL

Correct answer: C — C. UPDATE student SET name='abel' WHERE ID='123';

UPDATE needs SET to state the assignment and WHERE to restrict the target rows. C assigns the desired name only to the student with ID '123'. B changes IDs in every row, while D changes every student's name because it has no filter. A omits SET and is malformed. Correct syntax and the requested scope both matter.

Evidence in the complete study note

INSERT INTO Hotel(HotelID,HotelName,SubCity) VALUES (1,'Hilton','Yeka'); pairs each named column with a compatible value. Counts and types must match; keys and other constraints still apply. Without a column list, values must follow the table's declared column order.FDB29
UPDATE Book SET Price = 600 WHERE ID = 'B001'; changes matching rows.FDB29
Use single quotes for SQL string literals: WHERE ID = 'B001'.FDB28

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — SET is missing before the assignment.

B · incorrect — This sets every student's ID and does not change the name.

C · correct — SET changes name and WHERE restricts the operation to ID '123'.

D · incorrect — Without WHERE, every name is changed.

Question 43

A weak entity is identified by its partial key together with the key of an independently keyed associated entity. Which listed terms describe that associated entity set? Select all that apply.

  • A. Identifying set
  • B. Strong entity set
  • C. Neighbor set
  • D. Owner set

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 12

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check how a dependent entity obtains its complete identity.

Study-guide sections

FDB08 · Weak entities and identifying relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB08 · Weak entities and identifying relationships

Correct answers (select all): B, D — B. Strong entity set; D. Owner set

A weak entity cannot identify itself using its own attributes alone. Its owner contributes the missing key component, and in the specified case that owner has an independent key, so it is a strong entity set. Both owner set and strong entity set describe it. The identifying relationship connects the sets; neighbor does not identify the required ownership role.

Evidence in the complete study note

A weak entity lacks a complete independent key. Its owner is normally a strong entity, so owner entity set and strong entity set may both describe the associated set. Its partial key, also called discriminator, distinguishes weak entities of the same owner. Identification combines the owner key and partial key.FDB08
The weak entity participates totally in its identifying relationship.FDB08
Chen notation uses a double rectangle, double identifying diamond and dashed underline for a partial key.FDB08

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Identifying normally names the owner relationship rather than the defined owner entity-set role.

B · correct — The owner supplying the independent key is normally a strong entity set.

C · incorrect — Neighbor does not express the ownership and identification needed for a weak entity.

D · correct — Owner set names the entity set whose key contributes to weak-entity identification.

Question 45

In relational-table terminology, which listed terms name a unit of related field values treated together by an application? Select all that apply.

  • A. Rows
  • B. Field
  • C. Record
  • D. Database

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 20

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

Study-guide sections

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

Correct answers (select all): A, C — A. Rows; C. Record

A relational tuple groups related attribute values into one unit. Row and record are names for that same unit, so both options qualify even though one is plural. A field refers to one property rather than the whole grouping. A database is the larger coherent collection containing tables and their data, rather than one individual tuple.

Evidence in the complete study note

A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table. A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property. Degree counts columns; cardinality counts rows. Classical relations have distinct tuples, atomic cells and domains for attributes; row order has no meaning. Column order has no logical meaning when attributes are identified by name. SQL tables can contain duplicate rows unless constrained.FDB03
A database is a coherent collection of related facts with meaning for an application.FDB01
It controls redundancy rather than guaranteeing zero duplication.FDB01

Choice by choice

A · correct — A relational row groups related field values and is synonymous with a record.

B · incorrect — A field represents one attribute, not the collection of related fields in the definition.

C · correct — A record is the unit containing related fields described by the stem.

D · incorrect — A database contains a larger coherent collection, rather than this single field grouping.

Question 46

Which template correctly creates a table with one column? Treat table_name, column_name and column_type as placeholders for valid identifiers and a data type.

  • A. CREATE table_name (column_name, column type);
  • B. CREATE table_name (column_type, column name);
  • C. CREATE TABLE table_name (column_name column_type);
  • D. CREATE TABLE table_name (column_type, column_name);

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 40

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish table definition changes from changes to stored rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Correct answer: C — C. CREATE TABLE table_name (column_name column_type);

A column declaration places its name immediately before its data type. Commas separate complete declarations when more than one column is present. The corrected C uses CREATE TABLE and a properly paired name and type. A and B omit TABLE, while D separates and reverses the name/type parts rather than declaring the intended column.

Evidence in the complete study note

CREATE TABLE Employee (EmpID INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, Name VARCHAR(80)); declares two columns. The name and its type stay together; the comma separates whole column declarations. To add a primary key later: ALTER TABLE Employee ADD PRIMARY KEY (EmpID); existing data must be unique and non-NULL. A table cannot gain a second primary key without replacing the existing constraint.FDB30

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — TABLE is missing and the name/type pair is split by a comma.

B · incorrect — TABLE is missing and the type/name order is wrong.

C · correct — CREATE TABLE is followed by one correctly ordered name/type declaration.

D · incorrect — A comma separates type from name and no valid intended declaration is formed.
