Question 48

Consider a directed line(->) from the relationship set advisor to both entity sets instructor and student. This indicates cardinality

  • A. Many to one
  • B. Many to many
  • C. ¢. One to one
  • D. One to many

Source

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 56

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read the relationship in both directions and check participation constraints.

Study-guide sections

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Correct answer: C — One-to-one, under the common relationship-arrow convention.

FDB09 explicitly states the convention used for this example: arrows from advisor toward both Instructor and Student impose maximum-one at both ends. Each instructor can therefore be associated with at most one student and each student with at most one instructor. That is 1:1, without implying mandatory participation. A different notation would need an explicit legend.

Evidence in the complete study note

Read business rules in both directions. If each employee belongs to one department and each department has many employees, Employee→Department is N:1; Department→Employee is 1:N. Building→Location could also be N:1, so names alone cannot select a unique example. Under the common arrow convention, arrows from advisor to both Instructor and Student impose maximum-one at both ends, giving 1:1. Without a notation convention, arrows are unsafe evidence.FDB09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Many-to-one constrains only one end to maximum one, whereas both ends have arrows here.

B · incorrect — Many-to-many permits multiple associations at both ends, contrary to both maximum-one arrows.

C · correct — Maximum one at each end yields a one-to-one relationship under the stated common convention.

D · incorrect — One-to-many allows multiple associations at one end, which the second arrow rules out.

Question 49

What is the basic goal of Normalization?

  • A. To decrease data integrity
  • B. To reduce redundancy
  • C. ¢. To maximize transitive dependency
  • D. To increase dependency

Source

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 70

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.

Study-guide sections

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: B — To reduce redundancy

Normalization reduces repeated data and resulting anomalies.

Evidence in the complete study note

Suppose Enrollment repeats StudentName for every course. An update anomaly requires changing every copy of the student's name; missed copies disagree. An insertion anomaly prevents recording a new student until some enrollment exists. A deletion anomaly loses the only record of a student's details when their last enrollment is deleted. Revoke anomaly is not a normalization anomaly.FDB19
Reduced redundancy can improve integrity and storage use, but extra keys and joins mean neither storage savings nor faster retrieval is guaranteed.FDB19
Adding a generated row ID may make insertion technically possible, but does not remove repeated facts or fix the dependency design.FDB19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Integrity is protected (PDF 16.)

B · correct — Redundancy reduction is explicit (PDF 16.)

C · incorrect — 3NF removes transitive dependence (PDF 16.)

D · incorrect — Increasing dependency is not the goal (PDF 16.)

Question 50

For a relation with attributes ID, CITY and NAME, ID is guaranteed unique and non-NULL; CITY and NAME are not unique. Which listed attribute sets are superkeys? Select all that apply.

  • A. NAME
  • B. ID
  • C. CITY
  • D. CITY, ID

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 77

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Test uniqueness and minimality separately when classifying keys.

Study-guide sections

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answers (select all): B, D — B. ID; D. CITY, ID

A superkey is any attribute set guaranteed to identify a tuple; it need not be minimal. ID has the stated uniqueness guarantee, and adding CITY cannot destroy it. Thus ID and (CITY, ID) are superkeys. CITY and NAME individually lack uniqueness guarantees. Only ID is a minimal candidate key among the two qualifying choices, but minimality is not required here.

Evidence in the complete study note

A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple. A candidate key is a minimal superkey: removing any component loses uniqueness. Minimal means no unnecessary attribute, not necessarily the fewest columns among all keys. A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys. A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07
If ID is unique, both {ID} and {ID,City} are superkeys, but only {ID} is minimal.FDB07
Prefer stable, compact identifiers when practical, but Email, SSN or EmployeeID is a candidate only if business rules guarantee uniqueness.FDB07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Repeated names are permitted, so NAME is not a guaranteed identifier.

B · correct — ID is guaranteed unique and identifies each tuple.

C · incorrect — Repeated cities are permitted, so CITY alone is not a superkey.

D · correct — The set includes unique ID and therefore retains uniqueness.

Question 53

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: C — @) Database schema

Schema describes the designed database; instance and snapshot describe current data.

Evidence in the complete study note

A schema describes names, structures and constraints; an instance, state or snapshot is the data at a particular time. Inserting a student changes the state, while adding a column changes the schema. Question 54

Which listed terms denote database contents at one particular moment, rather than the database definition? Select all that apply.

  • B. Database state / snapshot
  • C. Database schema
  • D. None of the above

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answers (select all): A, B — A. Database instance; B. Database state / snapshot

An instance, state or snapshot describes the actual data at a selected time. Those contents can change after an insertion or update without changing the database definition. A schema describes names, structures and constraints instead. Therefore both the instance option and the state/snapshot option fit, while none of the above cannot be selected because these matching terms are present.

Evidence in the complete study note

A schema describes names, structures and constraints; an instance, state or snapshot is the data at a particular time. Inserting a student changes the state, while adding a column changes the schema. Metadata is data describing data. The catalog or data dictionary records table definitions, types, constraints and often access privileges and optimizer statistics. A schema diagram draws tables and key references; labels such as database diagram can overlap.FDB03

Choice by choice

C · incorrect — A schema describes structure and constraints rather than current data values.

Question 55

What is the capacity to change the conceptual schema while preserving affected external schemas and application interfaces?

  • A. Logical data independence
  • B. Physical data independence
  • C. Conceptual database
  • D. All of the above

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice questions · Question 15

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace which schema levels a proposed change affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Correct answer: A — Logical data independence

Logical data independence allows changes to the conceptual schema while preserving the external views and application interfaces that depend on it. Physical data independence instead concerns changing internal storage without changing the conceptual schema. A conceptual database or schema describes the logical structure itself; naming that structure does not express the ability to preserve external interfaces during a change. Consequently A names the requested property and the all-of-the-above choice is false.

Evidence in the complete study note

The external level gives each user group a selected view. The conceptual level describes the whole logical database: entity types, relationships, data types and constraints.FDB04
Physical data independence permits changing internal storage without changing the conceptual schema, for example adding an index. Logical data independence permits conceptual changes while preserving affected external views and application interfaces, for example splitting a table while maintaining a compatible view.FDB04

Choice by choice

A · correct — Correct: logical data independence preserves external interfaces across conceptual-schema changes.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: physical data independence concerns internal-storage changes while preserving the conceptual schema.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: conceptual database names the logical structure, not the property preserving external interfaces when that structure changes.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: physical independence and the conceptual database are not the requested change-preservation property.

  • B. ? | Data Definition Langugae (DDL) v
  • C. /@| Transaction Query Language (TQL) x
  • D. | None of the above x

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — ? | Data Definition Langugae (DDL) v

Defining a schema is definition rather than manipulation. DDL commands define structure, while DML commands manipulate data.

Question 57

Common functions applied to collections of numeric values: SUM, AVERAGE, COUNT. This function belongs to

  • A. Ww Grouping x
  • B. i? | Aggregate functions 4
  • C. |@| All of the above x
  • D. | None of the above ><

Source

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice questions · Question 18

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply grouping before filtering groups and computing aggregate output.

Study-guide sections

FDB32 · Aggregation, GROUP BY and HAVING

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB32 · Aggregation, GROUP BY and HAVING

Correct answer: B — Aggregate functions.

SUM, average and COUNT summarize collections into values, which is aggregation. GROUP BY instead partitions rows into groups for those summaries. SQL normally spells the average function AVG; the conceptual use of AVERAGE here still describes aggregation.

Evidence in the complete study note

COUNT(*) counts rows, COUNT(x) counts non-NULL x values; SUM and AVG summarize non-NULL numeric values. SQL's usual average function is AVG, not AVERAGE. GROUP BY creates one group per distinct grouping value; HAVING tests aggregate groups after WHERE has filtered individual rows.FDB32

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Grouping partitions rows; it is not the function category containing SUM and COUNT.

B · correct — These functions summarize a collection of values into a count, total or average.

C · incorrect — Grouping and aggregate functions are distinct concepts, so all listed categories cannot be correct.

D · incorrect — Aggregate functions is present and correctly describes the functions in the stem.

Question 58

Entity types that do not have key attributes of their own are called

  • A. a | Entity
  • B. i? | Strong Entity
  • C. @) Weak Entity
  • D. | Foreign Entity

FDB08 · Weak entities and identifying relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB08 · Weak entities and identifying relationships

Correct answer: C — @) Weak Entity

Weak entities lack their own key attributes, while strong entities have their own keys. Their partial keys identify them only within an owner.

Evidence in the complete study note

A weak entity lacks a complete independent key. Its owner is normally a strong entity, so owner entity set and strong entity set may both describe the associated set. Its partial key, also called discriminator, distinguishes weak entities of the same owner. Identification combines the owner key and partial key.FDB08
A weak entity is not merely any table with a foreign key.FDB08
Then (EmployeeID,DependentName) is the key, and EmployeeID is a foreign key.FDB08

Choice by choice

C · correct — Weak entity matches the exact definition. (PDF pp. 10.)

D · incorrect — Foreign entity is not the defined subtype; foreign keys concern table references. (PDF pp. 10.)

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

An entity may have physical existence or conceptual existence. Attributes describe an entity; relationships associate entity types. The stem asks for the object itself.

Question 61

In the usual normalization sequence, which is the first normal form that excludes partial dependencies of nonprime attributes on any candidate key?

  • A. First Normal Form (1NF)
  • B. Third Normal Form (3NF)
  • C. Second Normal Form (2NF)
  • D. Boyce–Codd Normal Form (BCNF)

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice questions · Question 24

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: C — Second Normal Form (2NF)

Second normal form is the first stage requiring atomic relations to have no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key. First normal form addresses atomic values, so it does not impose that dependency rule. Third normal form and BCNF are stronger stages that inherit the absence of partial dependencies, but they are not the first stage introducing it. BCNF specifically requires every nontrivial functional-dependency determinant to be a superkey.

Evidence in the complete study note

1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells.FDB20
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20
For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey. The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap. Remove dependencies of nonprime facts on nonkey determinants.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: 1NF requires atomic values but does not exclude partial dependencies.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: 3NF inherits 2NF and imposes its additional determinant/prime-attribute condition; it is not the first stage.

C · correct — Correct: 2NF is the first stage excluding nonprime partial dependencies on candidate keys.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: BCNF requires every nontrivial determinant to be a superkey; it is stronger than the first stage imposing the partial-dependency rule.

Question 62

In the usual normalization sequence, which is the first normal form that excludes transitive dependence of nonprime attributes on a key through a nonkey determinant?

  • A. Third Normal Form (3NF)
  • B. Fourth Normal Form (4NF)
  • C. First Normal Form (1NF)
  • D. All of the above

Adapted from

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice questions · Question 25

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: A — Third Normal Form (3NF)

In the stated single-key teaching case, 2NF excludes partial dependencies but can still allow a nonprime fact to depend on the key through a nonkey determinant. The next defining stage is 3NF, summarized by the whole-key-and-nothing-but-the-key mnemonic. BCNF strengthens the determinant rule, and 4NF addresses multivalued dependencies. Stronger forms inherit earlier conditions, so the explicit first-stage scope makes 3NF the unique selection rather than treating inherited properties as rival answers.

Evidence in the complete study note

1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells.FDB20
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20
For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey. The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap. Remove dependencies of nonprime facts on nonkey determinants.FDB21
4NF addresses nontrivial multivalued dependencies with nonsuperkey determinants; 5NF addresses join dependencies.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · correct — Correct: 3NF is the first stage imposing the specified nonprime transitive-dependency restriction in this teaching case.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: 4NF addresses multivalued dependencies and is a later stage; it is not the first stage imposing the 3NF condition.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: 1NF concerns atomic values and does not eliminate the specified transitive dependencies.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: Not all options identify the requested first stage: 1NF does not eliminate the specified transitive dependency and 4NF is later.

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

The conceptual schema describes the whole database while hiding storage details. External views show a particular group's part, and internal schema describes storage.

Question 64

Suppose you want to design a database for a certain company after you know the need of all users of the database. What is the next step you are expected to do?

  • A. Logical design
  • B. Normalization
  • C. Query Processing
  • D. Conceptual design

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 47

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the design phase from its inputs and intended deliverable.

Study-guide sections

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: D — Conceptual design

After collecting users' information requirements, conceptual design develops the ER/EER model that represents those needs. Logical design then maps that model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Normalization refines dependency structure rather than replacing the initial conceptual modeling step. Query processing evaluates database requests instead of defining the initial enterprise model. D is therefore the next step under the stated sequence, rather than a claim that design can never iterate.

Evidence in the complete study note

First collect business facts, required transactions, reports, volumes, privacy rules and quality targets. Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model. Logical design maps the conceptual model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical design chooses file organization, indexes and storage. Implementation creates and loads the actual objects; testing checks requirements.FDB05

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: logical design maps an already developed conceptual model into relations and constraints.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: normalization refines relation dependencies during logical design; the initial conceptual model must first be developed.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: query processing evaluates requests rather than creating the initial model from requirements.

D · correct — Correct: conceptual design follows the collection of requirements in the stated sequence.

Question 67

Which command is used to modifies an existing database object, such as a table?

  • A. Create
  • B. Alter
  • C. Drop
  • D. Modify

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 2

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish table definition changes from changes to stored rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — ALTER.

ALTER changes the definition of an existing database object. FDB30 demonstrates adding or removing columns and adding constraints with ALTER TABLE. CREATE establishes an object and DROP removes it. MODIFY can occur within some vendor-specific alteration syntax, but it is not the general top-level command requested here.

Evidence in the complete study note

ALTER TABLE Employee ADD COLUMN Email VARCHAR(120); adds a column in PostgreSQL-style syntax. ALTER TABLE Employee DROP COLUMN Email; removes it. ALTER TABLE Employee ADD CONSTRAINT uq_email UNIQUE (Email); adds a named constraint. Use ALTER rather than DALTER, and do not separate column_name and column_type with a comma; validate the resulting SQL before execution.FDB30
DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications.FDB26
Some texts separate SELECT as DQL.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — CREATE defines a new object rather than altering the definition of an existing one.

B · correct — ALTER is the command used to change an existing database object definition.

C · incorrect — DROP removes the object instead of modifying its existing definition.

D · incorrect — MODIFY is not the general standalone command; some dialects use it inside ALTER syntax.

Question 69

In the SQLite command-line shell, which command lists the names and files of attached databases?

  • A. db
  • B. use
  • C. .databases
  • D. database

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 4

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: C — C. .databases

The command belongs to the SQLite shell rather than ordinary SQL statement syntax. Its documented spelling is .databases, including the leading dot and final s, and it lists attached database names and files. The corrected C has that spelling. The alternatives db, use and database do not form this SQLite attached-database listing command.

Evidence in the complete study note

DELETE removes rows and keeps the table; DROP TABLE removes the table object; ALTER changes its definition. TRUNCATE empties a table using implementation-specific mechanisms. PostgreSQL allows transactional rollback of TRUNCATE, so no rollback information is retained is not a universal SQL property. SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific. SQLite's .databases lists attached names/files in its command-line shell; .database is not the documented command.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — db is not the SQLite shell listing command.

B · incorrect — use does not list SQLite attached names and files.

C · correct — The .databases command performs the requested listing.

D · incorrect — database lacks the required shell-command spelling.

Question 70

What is an RDBMS?

  • B. Database that accesses data elements that are not linked
  • C. Database that stores and allows access to data elements that are linked
  • D. None of the mentioned

Study-guide references

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answer: C — Database that stores and allows access to data elements that are linked

A relational DBMS manages a database represented by relations/tables, with references maintaining relationships between tuples. Among these broad descriptions C is closest, although linked data alone is not a complete definition distinguishing relational from other models.

Evidence in the complete study note

A data model supplies concepts for structure, relationships, constraints and operations. Conceptual ER models describe entities and relationships. Relational models use tables; hierarchical models organize records in a parent–child tree; classic network models connect records through pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. Object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. A physical model specifies record layouts and access paths. These are different representation choices, not stages of one query.FDB02

Question 71

What is a relation in RDBMS?

  • C. Row

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answer: B — Table

The relational model represents relations in table format. A tuple is one row; a key identifies tuples rather than being the relation itself.

Evidence in the complete study note

A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table. A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property. Degree counts columns; cardinality counts rows. Classical relations have distinct tuples, atomic cells and domains for attributes; row order has no meaning. A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple.FDB07
A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys.FDB07

Question 72

Relational Algebra is a __________ query language that takes two relations as input and produces another relation as an output of the query.

  • B. Structural
  • C. Procedural
  • D. Fundamental

Answer & Detailed Explanation

FDB36 · Relational calculus: state the desired facts

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: C — Procedural

Relational algebra is procedural because it specifies a sequence of operations. The stem overgeneralizes arity: operations can take one or more input relations, not always two.

Evidence in the complete study note

Relational algebra specifies a sequence of operations and is procedural at the logical level. Relational calculus specifies a predicate describing desired results and is declarative/nonprocedural. A tuple variable ranges over tuples; a domain variable ranges over individual attribute values. Safe/range-restricted expressions produce results bounded by the database's active domain.FDB36
Rename ρ changes relation or attribute names.FDB35

Question 73

Which of the following is a fundamental operation in relational algebra?

  • A. Set intersection
  • B. Natural join
  • C. Assignment
  • D. None of the mentioned

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB-S01 · Algebra operator lists and derivable operations

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: A — Set intersection

Under the stated Basic operations list, intersection is basic. A smaller primitive basis may instead treat intersection as derived; the answer uses the stated list.

Evidence in the complete study note

A list of basic relational-algebra operators is a teaching convention, distinct from a claim that every listed operation is logically indispensable. The course module uses selection, projection, Cartesian product, set difference, union and intersection as its basic-operation list. Natural join and assignment are not members of that particular list.FDB-S01
A smaller primitive basis may therefore omit intersection even when it is introduced as a basic operation.FDB-S01
Under set semantics, union includes tuples from either compatible input, intersection retains tuples common to both, and difference keeps first-input tuples absent from the second.FDB-S01

Question 74

Which of the following is used to denote the selection operation in relational algebra?

  • A. Pi (Greek)
  • B. Sigma (Greek)
  • C. Lambda (Greek)
  • D. Omega (Greek)

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 9

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply each algebra operator to the input relations step by step.

Study-guide sections

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answer: B — Sigma (Greek)

Selection filters tuples and is conventionally denoted by sigma, σ. Projection keeps attributes and is denoted by pi, π. Lambda and omega are different Greek symbols and therefore cannot identify the requested sigma operation under this notation. This is a question about the operator's conventional symbol, rather than whether another symbol could be assigned a new meaning in a custom notation. B is the unique selection symbol among the choices.

Evidence in the complete study note

Selection σ filters tuples; projection π retains attributes and removes duplicate tuples under set semantics.FDB35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: pi, π, denotes projection, which retains attributes.

B · correct — Correct: sigma, σ, denotes selection, which filters tuples.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: lambda, λ, is a different symbol from the conventional selection symbol σ.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: omega, ω, is a different symbol from the conventional selection symbol σ.

Question 75

For union-compatible relations, which listed names denote the minus operation R−S, returning tuples in R but not S? Select all that apply.

  • A. Union
  • B. Set-difference
  • C. Difference
  • D. Intersection

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 10

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Use the operator convention stated in the question and distinguish membership from derivability.

Study-guide sections

FDB-S01 · Algebra operator lists and derivable operations

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB-S01 · Algebra operator lists and derivable operations

FDB35 · Relational algebra: calculate results

Correct answers (select all): B, C — B. Set-difference; C. Difference

R−S tests membership asymmetrically: a tuple must occur in R and must not occur in S. Difference and set-difference are names for this same operator, so both should be selected. Union combines membership from either input, whereas intersection requires membership in both. Swapping the inputs to difference can change the result because the exclusion direction matters.

Evidence in the complete study note

Under set semantics, union includes tuples from either compatible input, intersection retains tuples common to both, and difference keeps first-input tuples absent from the second. Intersection can be expressed using difference, while a matching join can be expressed as selection over a Cartesian product. A smaller primitive basis may therefore omit intersection even when it is introduced as a basic operation.FDB-S01
Union, difference and intersection require union-compatible schemas.FDB35
Rename ρ changes relation or attribute names.FDB35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Union combines compatible input tuples rather than retaining only first-input tuples absent from the second.

B · correct — Set-difference returns tuples in the first relation that do not occur in the second.

C · correct — Difference is the same minus operator as set-difference.

D · incorrect — Intersection keeps tuples common to both relations, the opposite membership condition.

Question 76

Which one of the following is used to define the structure of the relation, deleting relations and relating schemas?

  • A. DML(Data Manipulation Langauge)
  • B. DDL(Data Definition Langauge)
  • C. Query
  • D. Relational Schema

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 11

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — DDL(Data Definition Langauge)

Defining and deleting relation structures are schema operations in DDL. DML operates on their data. Relating schemas here is interpreted as defining schema structure rather than retrieving joins.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — DML is for data operations. (PDF pp. 26.)

B · correct — DDL is the schema-definition category. (PDF pp. 26.)

C · incorrect — A query request is not the schema language category. (PDF pp. 26.)

D · incorrect — A schema is a description, not a language for changing it. (PDF pp. 26.)

Question 77

CREATE TABLE employee (name VARCHAR(80), id INTEGER); What kind of SQL statement is this?

  • A. DML
  • B. DDL
  • C. View
  • D. Integrity constraint

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 12

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Correct answer: B — DDL

CREATE TABLE defines a new table and its columns, so it is a data-definition-language statement. DML instead retrieves or changes stored rows. A view is a query-based logical object, while this statement creates a base table. Integrity constraints can be included inside a table definition, but this whole statement is not merely a constraint expression. VARCHAR has the stated length in this table definition.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26
CREATE TABLE Employee (EmpID INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, Name VARCHAR(80)); declares two columns. The name and its type stay together; the comma separates whole column declarations.FDB30

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: DML acts on stored data, whereas this statement defines a table.

B · correct — Correct: creating a table and declaring its columns is DDL.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: CREATE TABLE defines a table rather than a view.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: the statement defines an entire table, not just an integrity-constraint expression.

CHAR(n) is fixed length; VARCHAR(n) has variable length up to a limit. DATE represents a date and BLOB binary content such as images. Question 80

To remove a relation from an SQL database, we use the ______ comman d.

  • A. Delete
  • B. Purge
  • C. Remove
  • D. Drop table

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 15

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: D — DROP TABLE.

Removing a relation means removing the table object, rather than just its tuples. FDB26 distinguishes DROP TABLE from DELETE: DELETE preserves the table definition while removing rows. DROP TABLE removes the object itself. PURGE and REMOVE are not the general SQL command specified here for this task.

Evidence in the complete study note

DELETE removes rows and keeps the table; DROP TABLE removes the table object; ALTER changes its definition. TRUNCATE empties a table using implementation-specific mechanisms. PostgreSQL allows transactional rollback of TRUNCATE, so no rollback information is retained is not a universal SQL property. SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific. SQLite's .databases lists attached names/files in its command-line shell; .database is not the documented command.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — DELETE removes tuples while preserving the relation definition, so it does not remove the relation.

B · incorrect — PURGE is not the general table-removal command established here.

C · incorrect — REMOVE is not the SQL command used here to remove a relation object.

D · correct — DROP TABLE removes the table object, including its relation definition.

Question 81

Assume constraints permit the deletion. Which descriptions apply to executing DELETE FROM r; on an existing table r? Select all that apply.

  • A. Remove relation
  • B. Clear relation entries
  • C. Delete fields
  • D. Delete rows

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 16

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track which rows each data-changing command affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB29 · INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE

Correct answers (select all): B, D — B. Clear relation entries; D. Delete rows

Without WHERE, DELETE targets every row in r. If the operation succeeds, the table's entries are cleared but its column definitions and table object remain. Clear relation entries and delete rows describe this same effect. Removing the relation itself requires a schema operation such as DROP TABLE, while deleting fields would change columns rather than row contents.

Evidence in the complete study note

INSERT INTO Hotel(HotelID,HotelName,SubCity) VALUES (1,'Hilton','Yeka'); pairs each named column with a compatible value. Counts and types must match; keys and other constraints still apply. Without a column list, values must follow the table's declared column order.FDB29
Both clear relation entries and delete rows describe that effect.FDB29
DELETE FROM r; removes every row but leaves r defined.FDB29

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The table definition remains; DELETE does not remove the relation object.

B · correct — Without WHERE, every relation entry is removed while the table remains available.

C · incorrect — Fields are columns; DELETE removes rows rather than column definitions.

D · correct — Every row is targeted because the statement contains no filtering WHERE clause.

Question 83

Which joins discard unmatched tuples from both inputs? Interpret Natural join as an ordinary natural inner join. Select all that apply.

  • A. Left outer join
  • B. Right outer join
  • C. Inner join
  • D. Natural join

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 18

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute matching pairs, then account for any preserved unmatched rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Correct answers (select all): C, D — C. Inner join; D. Natural join

An inner join returns only pairs that satisfy its matching condition, so unmatched tuples from either input disappear. An ordinary natural join is an inner equality join using all common column names and has the same preservation behavior. Left and right outer joins instead preserve unmatched tuples from their designated side by adding NULLs for the other side.

Evidence in the complete study note

An inner join returns matching pairs satisfying ON. An equijoin uses equality; a theta join permits another comparison. A natural join is ordinarily an inner equality join on every same-named column and merges those duplicate columns. JOIN … USING (DeptID) specifies shared join columns explicitly. CROSS JOIN forms every pair without a matching condition.FDB31
LEFT OUTER JOIN also preserves unmatched left rows, padding right columns with NULL; RIGHT preserves right rows and FULL preserves both.FDB31
Therefore inner and natural inner joins both discard unmatched rows.FDB31

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A left outer join preserves unmatched left rows, filling right-side columns with NULL.

B · incorrect — A right outer join preserves unmatched right rows, filling left-side columns with NULL.

C · correct — An inner join returns matching pairs only, so unmatched tuples do not survive.

D · correct — An ordinary natural join is an inner equality join and discards unmatched tuples.

Question 84

What type of join is needed when you wish to include rows that do not have matching values?

  • A. Equi-join
  • B. Natural join
  • C. Outer join
  • D. All of the mentioned

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 19

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute matching pairs, then account for any preserved unmatched rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Correct answer: C — Outer join.

An outer join retains unmatched rows from one or both inputs and fills the missing side with NULL. FDB31 contrasts that behavior with ordinary matching-only joins. Equijoin describes an equality condition, and an ordinary natural join is an inner equality join; neither alone guarantees unmatched-row preservation. Therefore all of the mentioned is false.

Evidence in the complete study note

An inner join returns matching pairs satisfying ON. An equijoin uses equality; a theta join permits another comparison. A natural join is ordinarily an inner equality join on every same-named column and merges those duplicate columns. JOIN … USING (DeptID) specifies shared join columns explicitly. CROSS JOIN forms every pair without a matching condition.FDB31
Therefore inner and natural inner joins both discard unmatched rows.FDB31
LEFT OUTER JOIN also preserves unmatched left rows, padding right columns with NULL; RIGHT preserves right rows and FULL preserves both.FDB31

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — An equality condition alone does not retain rows that fail to match.

B · incorrect — An ordinary natural join uses matching shared-column values and discards unmatched rows.

C · correct — Outer joins preserve unmatched rows from the left, right or both inputs.

D · incorrect — The other named joins do not all guarantee preservation of unmatched rows.

Question 85

How many input relations does one ordinary binary join operator combine? Count operator inputs, not distinct base tables used by a complete query.

  • A. One
  • B. Two
  • C. Three
  • D. All of the mentioned

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 20

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute matching pairs, then account for any preserved unmatched rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Correct answer: B — B. Two

One binary join operator combines two input relations. Either input may itself be the output of an earlier join, so the complete query can involve more base tables. A self-join can refer twice to one base table, but still supplies two relation occurrences to the operator. The question fixes the count at the operator level, making two the unique choice.

Evidence in the complete study note

An inner join returns matching pairs satisfying ON. An equijoin uses equality; a theta join permits another comparison. A natural join is ordinarily an inner equality join on every same-named column and merges those duplicate columns. JOIN … USING (DeptID) specifies shared join columns explicitly. CROSS JOIN forms every pair without a matching condition.FDB31
Each ordinary join operator combines two inputs, but its inputs can be earlier joins, so one query can join many tables.FDB31
Distinguish base-table count, table occurrences and binary operator arity.FDB31

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — One distinct base table can support a self-join, but the operator still has two inputs.

B · correct — A binary join operator has exactly two inputs.

C · incorrect — Three base tables require a chain of binary joins, not one operator.

D · incorrect — The input count of the specified binary operator is fixed, so all counts do not apply.

Question 86

Which option gives the complete set of valid SQL join forms among the listed alternatives? Choose the most complete single answer.

  • A. Cross join
  • B. Natural join
  • C. Join with USING clause
  • D. All of the mentioned

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 21

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute matching pairs, then account for any preserved unmatched rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB31 · Join behavior and preservation

Correct answer: D — All of the mentioned

CROSS JOIN forms every pair of input rows without a matching predicate. NATURAL JOIN matches all same-named columns and merges the duplicate join columns. JOIN USING explicitly names shared equality columns. All three are valid join forms, although they do not all express a join condition in the same way. The revised stem requests the complete single answer, so the aggregate D is selected; each individually true alternative is incomplete on its own.

Evidence in the complete study note

An inner join returns matching pairs satisfying ON. An equijoin uses equality; a theta join permits another comparison. A natural join is ordinarily an inner equality join on every same-named column and merges those duplicate columns. JOIN … USING (DeptID) specifies shared join columns explicitly. CROSS JOIN forms every pair without a matching condition.FDB31

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect as the complete selection: CROSS JOIN is a valid join form without a matching condition, but B and C also qualify.

B · incorrect — Incorrect as the complete selection: NATURAL JOIN is valid and matches same-named columns, but A and C also qualify.

C · incorrect — Incorrect as the complete selection: JOIN USING is valid and names shared equality columns, but A and B also qualify.

D · correct — Correct: all three named constructs are valid SQL join forms, so the aggregate is the complete answer.

Question 88

A many to many relationship between two entities usually results in how many tables?

  • A. One
  • B. Two
  • C. Three
  • D. Four

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify relationship degree and cardinality before choosing a relational mapping.

Study-guide sections

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

Correct answer: C — Three tables: two entity tables and one association table.

Each of the two entity types normally has its own relation. Their many-to-many relationship requires an additional association relation holding references to both entities and any relationship attributes. FDB12 illustrates Student, Course and Enrollment. Thus the usual total is three, rather than only the one new association table.

Evidence in the complete study note

A binary M:N relationship needs an association table in addition to the two entity tables. Enrollment(StudentID,CourseID,Grade) references Student and Course; (StudentID,CourseID) is a key if one enrollment per pair is allowed. Repeated attempts require Term or AttemptNo in the key. This ordinarily produces three tables.FDB12

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — One counts only the association table and omits the two participating entity tables.

B · incorrect — Two entity tables alone do not record arbitrary many-to-many pairs.

C · correct — The two entity tables plus their association table produce the usual total of three.

D · incorrect — No fourth table is required by the ordinary binary many-to-many mapping.

Question 89

An ________ is a set of entities of the same type that share the same properties, or attributes.

  • A. Entity set
  • B. Attribute set
  • C. Relation set
  • D. Entity model

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Review entity, attribute and relationship notation before interpreting the model.

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: A — Entity set.

FDB06 distinguishes an individual entity from the type describing its properties and the set containing the current entities of that type. The stem asks for that collection of similarly described entities, which is an entity set. Attributes describe properties; a model describes representation rather than the current collection itself.

Evidence in the complete study note

An entity is a distinguishable real or conceptual thing, such as one student or one course. An entity type describes common properties; its current collection of entities is an entity set. A relationship associates entities. In Chen notation rectangles represent entity types, diamonds relationships and ellipses attributes; double ellipses denote multivalued attributes and dashed ellipses derived attributes. Always inspect the diagram legend.FDB06
A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table.FDB03
A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property.FDB03

Choice by choice

A · correct — An entity set is the collection of entities belonging to one common entity type.

B · incorrect — An attribute set groups properties, rather than the entities possessing those properties.

C · incorrect — Relation set is not the ER term for the stated collection of entities.

D · incorrect — An entity model describes structures, rather than naming the current set of entity instances.

Question 91

E-R modeling technique is a :

  • A. Top-down approach
  • B. Bottom-up approach
  • C. Left-right approach
  • D. None of the above

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 26

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the design phase from its inputs and intended deliverable.

Study-guide sections

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Correct answer: A — Top-down is the usual conceptual ER approach.

FDB05 describes conceptual design as usually starting with an enterprise view and working downward into entity and relationship details. This is the usual top-down classification of ER modeling. Bottom-up refinement is associated here with normalization from dependencies. This is a useful conventional approach, rather than a claim that every ER modeling activity must follow one direction.

Evidence in the complete study note

First collect business facts, required transactions, reports, volumes, privacy rules and quality targets. Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model. Logical design maps the conceptual model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical design chooses file organization, indexes and storage. Implementation creates and loads the actual objects; testing checks requirements.FDB05
Conceptual design usually proceeds from a whole enterprise view downward; normalization can refine structures from dependencies upward.FDB05
These are useful approaches rather than mandatory directions for every modeling activity.FDB05

Choice by choice

A · correct — Conceptual ER modeling usually moves from an overall enterprise view toward detailed entities and relationships.

B · incorrect — Bottom-up dependency refinement describes normalization here, rather than the usual ER conceptual approach.

C · incorrect — Left-right is not a database design methodology identified here.

D · incorrect — Top-down is an available and appropriate description of the usual conceptual ER approach.

Question 92

The number of entities to which another entity can be associated via a relationship set is expressed as :

  • A. Entity
  • B. Cardinality
  • C. Schema
  • D. Attributes

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 27

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read the relationship in both directions and check participation constraints.

Study-guide sections

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Correct answer: B — Cardinality

Cardinality measures how often an entity participates and distinguishes one-to-one, one-to-many, and many-to-many. That is the number of possible associations asked about.

Evidence in the complete study note

Cardinality specifies maximum associated occurrences: 1:1, 1:N or M:N. Minimum cardinality specifies optional/partial participation (zero permitted) or mandatory/total participation (at least one). A maximum of one does not mean participation is compulsory. Relationship degree counts participating entity roles: unary recursive, binary or ternary.FDB09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Entity is a participating object. (PDF pp. 11, 12.)

B · correct — Cardinality is the association-count constraint. (PDF pp. 11, 12.)

C · incorrect — Schema describes structure rather than the count. (PDF pp. 11, 12.)

D · incorrect — Attributes are properties, not the association-count classification. (PDF pp. 11, 12.)

Question 93

Relations produced from an E-R model will always be in :

  • A. First normal form
  • B. Second normal form
  • C. Third normal form
  • D. Fourth normal form

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 28

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: A — First normal form, assuming correct ER-to-relational mapping.

FDB20 states that properly splitting composite and multivalued ER structures produces ordinary 1NF relations. Nothing in an ER drawing alone proves absence of partial, transitive or multivalued dependencies needed for higher forms. Thus A is the guaranteed level under correct relational mapping; the word always must not be read as excusing an incorrect mapping.

Evidence in the complete study note

1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells. It does not mean merely declaring a primary key. A mapped ER design that correctly splits composite and multivalued structures gives ordinary 1NF tables; ER drawing alone guarantees no higher form.FDB20
Higher forms inherit 2NF, so no partial dependencies establishes the minimum guaranteed stage only when 1NF is also assumed.FDB20
A relation whose every candidate key is single-attribute has no nonprime partial-key dependency.FDB20

Choice by choice

A · correct — Correctly mapped atomic relations are in 1NF; this is the guaranteed level described here.

B · incorrect — An ER model alone does not rule out partial dependencies on a composite candidate key.

C · incorrect — An ER model alone does not establish the full dependency conditions required for 3NF.

D · incorrect — An ER model alone does not rule out the nontrivial multivalued dependencies addressed by 4NF.

Question 94

In ER model the details of the entities are hidden from the user. This process is called :

  • A. generalization
  • B. specialization
  • C. abstraction
  • D. none of these above

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

FDB13 · EER inheritance and category constraints

Correct answer: C — abstraction

Abstraction hides unwanted or irrelevant details. Generalization groups subclasses; specialization divides a superclass. Thus hiding entity details describes abstraction.

Evidence in the complete study note

The external level gives each user group a selected view. The conceptual level describes the whole logical database: entity types, relationships, data types and constraints. The internal/physical level describes files, record placement and access paths. These are abstraction levels, distinct from client/server deployment tiers.FDB04
Specialization divides Student into Undergraduate and Graduate; generalization combines common properties into Student.FDB13
EER extends ER with superclass/subclass structure.FDB13

D · incorrect — The required term is present. (PDF pp. 6, 13.)

Question 95

Study-guide references

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

Correct answer: A — Composite attribute

Composite attributes have smaller meaningful subparts. This differs from multiple values of one attribute and from calculating a value from another.

Evidence in the complete study note

Simple versus composite asks whether a value has meaningful components: Address may split into City and Street. Single-valued versus multivalued asks how many values one entity may have: a student may have several phone numbers. These dimensions differ: one address may be composite and single-valued. D · incorrect — Multivalued concerns a set of values rather than components of one value. (PDF pp. 10, 10.)

Question 99

An association of several entities in an Entity-Relation model is called :

  • A. Tuple
  • B. Record
  • C. Relationship
  • D. Field

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB06 · Entities, attributes and ER notation

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: C — Relationship

A relationship represents an association between entity types. Tuples and records instead represent individual table rows.

Evidence in the complete study note

An entity is a distinguishable real or conceptual thing, such as one student or one course. An entity type describes common properties; its current collection of entities is an entity set. A relationship associates entities. In Chen notation rectangles represent entity types, diamonds relationships and ellipses attributes; double ellipses denote multivalued attributes and dashed ellipses derived attributes. Always inspect the diagram legend.FDB06
A tuple/row/record contains related attribute values; an attribute/column/field describes a property.FDB03
A relation is a set of tuples, represented as a table.FDB03

D · incorrect — Field is an attribute component, not the association. (PDF pp. 11, 13.)

Question 100

To include integrity constraint in an existing relation use:

  • A. Modify table
  • B. Drop table
  • C. Alter table
  • D. Create table

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 35

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish table definition changes from changes to stored rows.

Study-guide sections

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: C — Alter table

Adding an integrity constraint to an existing table changes its definition, so ALTER TABLE is the appropriate command family. It can add a named constraint or a primary key, provided existing data satisfies the rule. DROP TABLE removes the object, and CREATE TABLE defines a new one. MODIFY TABLE does not follow the ALTER TABLE command form required for this structural change. The remaining constraint clause depends on the integrity rule to be enforced.

Evidence in the complete study note

ALTER TABLE Employee ADD COLUMN Email VARCHAR(120); adds a column in PostgreSQL-style syntax. ALTER TABLE Employee DROP COLUMN Email; removes it. ALTER TABLE Employee ADD CONSTRAINT uq_email UNIQUE (Email); adds a named constraint. Use ALTER rather than DALTER, and do not separate column_name and column_type with a comma; validate the resulting SQL before execution.FDB30
DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: the required command family for this definition change is ALTER TABLE, not MODIFY TABLE.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: DROP TABLE removes the existing object instead of adding a constraint.

C · correct — Correct: ALTER TABLE can add a constraint to an existing table definition.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: CREATE TABLE defines a new table rather than changing this existing one.

Question 101

Which option is not a general SQL integrity-constraint keyword or clause? Distinguish a constraint clause from a business condition that could be expressed inside one.

  • A. Not null
  • B. Positive
  • C. Unique
  • D. Check predicate

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 36

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read each SQL constraint's scope and enforcement rule.

Study-guide sections

FDB16 · Write and enforce SQL constraints

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB16 · Write and enforce SQL constraints

Correct answer: B — Positive

NOT NULL forbids missing values, UNIQUE enforces uniqueness under the DBMS's NULL rules, and CHECK evaluates a declared predicate. POSITIVE by itself is not a general SQL constraint keyword. A positivity requirement is still a valid business rule and can be written as a CHECK predicate such as CHECK (Amount > 0). The question therefore distinguishes clause syntax from the meaning of a possible rule, making B the unique incorrect keyword rather than rejecting positivity as an integrity requirement.

Evidence in the complete study note

Example: CREATE TABLE Enrollment (StudentID INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES Student(StudentID), CourseID INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES Course(CourseID), Grade INTEGER CHECK (Grade BETWEEN 0 AND 100), PRIMARY KEY (StudentID,CourseID)); Each column declaration gives its name then type, with commas between declarations. CHECK is a constraint clause, not a standalone GRANT-like statement. POSITIVE is not a general SQL constraint keyword; CHECK (Amount > 0) expresses positivity.FDB16
PRIMARY KEY implies uniqueness and non-NULL. UNIQUE does not generally imply NOT NULL; NULL treatment differs by DBMS.FDB16

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect selection: NOT NULL is a constraint that forbids NULL values.

B · correct — Correct selection: POSITIVE is not the standalone constraint keyword; a CHECK predicate can enforce positivity.

C · incorrect — Incorrect selection: UNIQUE is a constraint enforcing uniqueness.

D · incorrect — Incorrect selection: CHECK is the clause for enforcing a declared predicate.

Question 102

Foreign key is the one in which the ________ of one relation is referenced in another relation.

  • A. Foreign key
  • B. Primary key
  • C. References
  • D. Check constraint

Source

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 37

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answer: B — Primary key

A foreign key references a matching candidate, usually primary, key in the parent. B is the offered conventional primary-key case; the definition also permits a candidate key, so primary is not an exclusive universal requirement.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — An arbitrary foreign key is not the required parent unique target. (PDF pp. 48.)

B · correct — Primary key is the listed usual referenced key. (PDF pp. 48.)

C · incorrect — References names a mechanism rather than the key category. (PDF pp. 48.)

D · incorrect — A CHECK predicate is not the referenced key. (PDF pp. 48.)

Question 103

Which are uses of data integrity constraints, rather than access authorization? Select all that apply.

  • A. Control who is allowed access to the data
  • B. Ensure that duplicate records are not entered into the table
  • C. Improve the quality of data entered for a specific property (i.e., table column)
  • D. Prevent users from changing the values stored in the table

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 38

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Match the validity requirement to the appropriate constraint definition.

Study-guide sections

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answers (select all): B, C — B. Ensure that duplicate records are not entered into the table; C. Improve the quality of data entered for a specific property (i.e., table column)

Integrity constraints test whether data is valid according to the schema's rules. Uniqueness constraints can reject duplicate keyed records, while domain constraints reject inappropriate values for a column. These support the two selected purposes. Authorization controls who may access the data. Integrity rejects invalid changes rather than prohibiting all changes, so it does not imply that every stored value is immutable.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Controlling who may access data is authorization rather than data integrity itself.

B · correct — Key or uniqueness constraints prevent duplicate keyed records.

C · correct — Domain constraints validate types and allowed values for a specific column.

D · incorrect — Integrity permits valid updates; it does not prohibit every change to stored values.

Question 105

Customer has candidate keys CustId, Email and ContactNo. CustId is an immutable compact generated identifier; Email and ContactNo may change. A policy prefers stable compact candidate keys. Which is the best primary key under that policy?

  • A. CustId
  • B. Email
  • C. ContactNo
  • D. CustId and ContactNo

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 40

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Test uniqueness and minimality separately when classifying keys.

Study-guide sections

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answer: A — A. CustId

Every candidate key is eligible for primary-key selection, but the policy gives a reason to prefer one. CustId is explicitly stable and compact, while the contact attributes may change. Choosing CustId follows that policy without denying the uniqueness of the other candidates. Combining CustId and ContactNo is nonminimal because CustId alone already identifies each customer.

Evidence in the complete study note

A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple. A candidate key is a minimal superkey: removing any component loses uniqueness. Minimal means no unnecessary attribute, not necessarily the fewest columns among all keys. A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys. A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07
Prefer stable, compact identifiers when practical, but Email, SSN or EmployeeID is a candidate only if business rules guarantee uniqueness.FDB07
A table can have several candidates and foreign keys but at most one declared primary-key constraint, possibly containing several columns.FDB07

Choice by choice

A · correct — Its stated stability and compactness satisfy the preference policy.

B · incorrect — Email is eligible but may change, so it loses under this policy.

C · incorrect — ContactNo is eligible but may change, so it loses under this policy.

D · incorrect — Adding ContactNo to an already unique CustId is unnecessary and nonminimal.

FDB16 · Write and enforce SQL constraints

The designer selects one candidate key as the primary key; a composite primary key is still one key. No numeric-only restriction appears: a key is defined by uniqueness, not by a prohibition on string type. Thus both A and B are false claims.

A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple. A candidate key is a minimal superkey: removing any component loses uniqueness. Question 107

Which is the subset of SQL commands used to manipulate Oracle Database structures, including tables?

  • C. DML and DDL

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: A — Data Definition Language(DDL)

Schema-structure manipulation falls under definition language, despite the everyday word manipulate. DML is the separate category for data manipulation.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Question 108

Using the course's textbook SQL command categories, with RENAME treated as DDL, which listed commands are outside DDL? Select all that apply.

  • A. RENAME
  • B. REVOKE
  • C. GRANT
  • D. UPDATE

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 43

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answers (select all): B, C, D — B. REVOKE; C. GRANT; D. UPDATE

DDL concerns structural definitions and includes RENAME in this course classification. GRANT and REVOKE manage privileges, placing them in DCL, while UPDATE changes existing data and belongs to DML. All three are consequently outside DDL. Classifying by each statement's effect distinguishes schema operations, access permissions and data changes even though all are expressed using SQL.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — RENAME is commonly classified as DDL in the note's textbook taxonomy.

B · correct — REVOKE removes privileges and is DCL rather than DDL.

C · correct — GRANT assigns privileges and is DCL rather than DDL.

D · correct — UPDATE changes data and is DML rather than DDL.

Question 109

Which command empties an existing table while retaining its definition, using DBMS-specific table-emptying mechanisms rather than asserting that rollback is impossible?

  • A. TRUNCATE command
  • B. Create command
  • C. Drop table command
  • D. Alter table command

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 44

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: A — A. TRUNCATE command

TRUNCATE empties a table while retaining the table object. Its implementation, logging and transaction behavior depend on the DBMS, so the answer does not rely on a claim that rollback is impossible. CREATE defines an object, DROP TABLE removes it, and ALTER changes its definition. Only TRUNCATE has the requested table-emptying role among these choices.

Evidence in the complete study note

DELETE removes rows and keeps the table; DROP TABLE removes the table object; ALTER changes its definition. TRUNCATE empties a table using implementation-specific mechanisms. PostgreSQL allows transactional rollback of TRUNCATE, so no rollback information is retained is not a universal SQL property. SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific. SQLite's .databases lists attached names/files in its command-line shell; .database is not the documented command.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · correct — TRUNCATE empties the table and keeps its definition.

B · incorrect — CREATE defines objects rather than emptying an existing table.

C · incorrect — DROP TABLE removes the definition as well as the table.

D · incorrect — ALTER changes the table definition rather than serving as the table-emptying command.

Question 110

Which SQL command form removes a database object together with its contained database objects?

  • A. DROP DATABASE
  • B. DELETE DATABASE
  • C. REMOVE DATABASE
  • D. None of the mentioned

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 45

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: A — DROP DATABASE

DROP removes a database object, whereas DELETE removes rows from an existing table. DROP DATABASE is consequently the correct command form for removing a database and its contained objects. DELETE DATABASE does not express that structural operation, and REMOVE DATABASE does not follow the taught DROP syntax. Since the correct DROP DATABASE form is one of the options, the none-of-the-mentioned alternative is also incorrect.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26
SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · correct — Correct: DROP DATABASE removes the database object and its contained objects.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: DELETE is row removal; deleting rows does not remove the database object.

C · incorrect — Incorrect: the database-removal command form uses DROP, not REMOVE.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: DROP DATABASE is present and performs the requested structural operation.

Question 111

In SQL Server, which statement drops the two databases named Sales and suppliers? Assume permissions and database state allow the operation.

  • A. DROP DATABASE Sales, NewSales
  • B. DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers
  • C. DROP DATABASE Sales and suppliers
  • D. DROP DATABASE Sales-suppliers

Adapted from

  • Database Fundamentals practice questions · Question 46

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Classify each SQL command by the kind of change it performs.

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: B — B. DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers

SQL Server permits a comma-separated list of database names after DROP DATABASE. B names exactly Sales and suppliers using that syntax. A names a different second database, while AND and a hyphen do not form the required two-name list. The answer is specific to SQL Server and does not assert that the same multi-database syntax works in every SQL dialect.

Evidence in the complete study note

DELETE removes rows and keeps the table; DROP TABLE removes the table object; ALTER changes its definition. TRUNCATE empties a table using implementation-specific mechanisms. PostgreSQL allows transactional rollback of TRUNCATE, so no rollback information is retained is not a universal SQL property. SQL Server permits DROP DATABASE Sales, suppliers; that comma-list syntax is vendor-specific. SQLite's .databases lists attached names/files in its command-line shell; .database is not the documented command.FDB26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The second target is NewSales rather than suppliers.

B · correct — The supported comma list names both required databases.

C · incorrect — AND is not the separator in this DROP DATABASE syntax.

D · incorrect — A hyphen does not separate these two database names.

Question 112

Command that is used to add attributes to an existing relation, is said to be

  • A. Alter
  • B. Modify
  • C. Tailor
  • D. Eliminate

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB30 · CREATE and ALTER tables

Correct answer: A — ALTER.

Adding attributes changes a relation schema. FDB30 demonstrates ALTER TABLE Employee ADD COLUMN Email VARCHAR(120), keeping the column name next to its type. ALTER is therefore the required command family. The remaining alternatives do not name the general SQL schema-change command shown by the complete note.

Evidence in the complete study note

ALTER TABLE Employee ADD CONSTRAINT uq_email UNIQUE (Email); adds a named constraint. Use ALTER rather than DALTER, and do not separate column_name and column_type with a comma; validate the resulting SQL before execution.FDB30
