Question 170

What is denormalization in database design?

  • A. The process of breaking down a complex table into smaller tables
  • B. The process of adding redundancy to improve data retrieval performance
  • C. The process of eliminating duplicate values from a table
  • D. The process of converting data into a different format for storage

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 140

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare design techniques by the specific problem each solves.

Study-guide sections

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

Correct answer: B — Deliberately adding redundancy to improve selected retrieval workloads.

FDB38 defines denormalization as deliberately repeating or precomputing facts to reduce selected read costs, with extra maintenance work. It is a workload-driven trade-off rather than elimination of all duplication. Breaking tables apart typically describes normalization; changing storage format is a different concern. Thus B captures both the mechanism and its intended retrieval benefit.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normalization reduces dependency-driven anomalies. Indexing supplies search paths. Denormalization deliberately repeats or precomputes facts to reduce selected read costs, increasing maintenance work. Partitioning divides rows or columns; replication creates copies to improve locality or availability while complicating updates. These techniques can coexist. An e-commerce or gaming label alone does not select one.FDB38

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Splitting tables according to dependencies is normalization, rather than the redundancy introduced by denormalization.

B · correct — Denormalization deliberately repeats or precomputes information to improve selected retrieval workloads.

C · incorrect — Eliminating duplication describes a different direction from deliberately introducing useful redundant facts.

D · incorrect — Changing storage format does not define the dependency and redundancy trade-off of denormalization.

Question 171

What is the purpose of a view in a database?

  • A. To define the structure of a table
  • B. To store complex queries for reuse
  • C. To encrypt sensitive data
  • D. To enforce data types in columns

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 141

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a stored query definition from stored data and trace subquery scope.

Study-guide sections

FDB33 · Subqueries and views

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB33 · Subqueries and views

Correct answer: B — Store a query definition for reuse as a virtual table.

FDB33 says CREATE VIEW stores a query definition and presents a virtual table. B describes reusing that query, although a view need not be complex. Ordinary views do not inherently store result rows. Table definitions and column types belong to schema DDL, and views do not encrypt sensitive content merely by hiding columns or rows.

Evidence in the complete study note

A subquery is a SELECT nested inside another statement. It may appear in WHERE, HAVING or FROM, not only the first two. IN tests membership; EXISTS tests whether at least one qualifying row exists. Correlated subqueries refer to the outer row. A scalar subquery must produce an appropriate single value. Beware NOT IN when its subquery can return NULL; NOT EXISTS often expresses the intended absence more safely.FDB33
CREATE VIEW CSStudent AS SELECT StudentID,Name FROM Student WHERE Major = 'CS'; stores a query definition and presents a virtual table.FDB33
An ordinary view does not necessarily store its result; a materialized view does.FDB33

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A view defines a virtual query result rather than the base table's storage structure.

B · correct — A view saves a query definition that can be reused as a virtual table.

C · incorrect — Restricting exposed data through a view does not encrypt that data.

D · incorrect — Column data types are enforced by schema definitions and constraints rather than the view's primary purpose.

Question 172

Which database model represents data as a collection of objects?

  • A. Hierarchical model
  • B. Relational model
  • C. Object-oriented model
  • D. Network model

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 142

Compare the data models by how they organize records and relationships.

Study-guide sections

FDB02 · Data models and product names

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB02 · Data models and product names

Correct answer: C — Object-oriented model.

FDB02 says object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. That matches a collection of objects directly. Hierarchical models use a parent-child tree, relational models use tables, and classic network models link records through owner/member pointers. Those representations should not be conflated merely because programming languages can wrap their data in objects.

Evidence in the complete study note

A data model supplies concepts for structure, relationships, constraints and operations. Conceptual ER models describe entities and relationships. Relational models use tables; hierarchical models organize records in a parent–child tree; classic network models connect records through pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. Object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. A physical model specifies record layouts and access paths. These are different representation choices, not stages of one query.FDB02

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The hierarchical model organizes records in a parent-child tree rather than primarily as objects.

B · incorrect — The relational model represents data in tables with tuples and attributes.

C · correct — The object model represents objects with identity, state and behavior.

D · incorrect — The network model represents linked records through owner/member connections rather than object behavior.

Question 173

What is the purpose of data normalization in database design?

  • A. To improve data storage efficiency
  • B. To simplify the database structure
  • C. To eliminate data redundancy
  • D. To optimize query performance

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 143

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.

Study-guide sections

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: C — To eliminate data redundancy

Normalization organizes/decomposes tables to avoid redundancy and anomalies. Storage may benefit, but the explicit defining purpose among these choices is reducing/eliminating repeated data.

Evidence in the complete study note

Suppose Enrollment repeats StudentName for every course. An update anomaly requires changing every copy of the student's name; missed copies disagree. An insertion anomaly prevents recording a new student until some enrollment exists. A deletion anomaly loses the only record of a student's details when their last enrollment is deleted. Revoke anomaly is not a normalization anomaly.FDB19
Adding a generated row ID may make insertion technically possible, but does not remove repeated facts or fix the dependency design.FDB19
Normalization separates facts according to their determinants, reducing avoidable repetition and associated anomalies.FDB19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Storage efficiency can be a consequence, but the cited definition does not guarantee a storage result. (PDF pp. 16.)

B · incorrect — Decomposition does not necessarily simplify the whole schema. (PDF pp. 16.)

C · correct — Eliminating redundancy is explicitly stated. (PDF pp. 16.)

D · incorrect — Query speed is not the stated normalization goal. (PDF pp. 16.)

Question 174

In the customary single-key teaching case, which is the first normalization stage summarized as dependence on the key, the whole key, and nothing but the key?

  • A. First normal form (1NF)
  • B. Second normal form (2NF)
  • C. Third normal form (3NF)
  • D. Boyce-Codd normal form (BCNF)

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 143

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: C — Third normal form (3NF)

In the stated single-key teaching case, 2NF excludes partial dependencies but can still allow a nonprime fact to depend on the key through a nonkey determinant. The next defining stage is 3NF, summarized by the whole-key-and-nothing-but-the-key mnemonic. BCNF strengthens the determinant rule, and 4NF addresses multivalued dependencies. Stronger forms inherit earlier conditions, so the explicit first-stage scope makes 3NF the unique selection rather than treating inherited properties as rival answers.

Evidence in the complete study note

1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells.FDB20
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20
For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey. The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap. Remove dependencies of nonprime facts on nonkey determinants.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: 1NF concerns atomic values and does not eliminate the specified transitive dependencies.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: 2NF excludes partial dependencies but can retain a nonprime transitive dependency through a nonkey determinant.

C · correct — Correct: 3NF is the first stage imposing the specified nonprime transitive-dependency restriction in this teaching case.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: BCNF strengthens 3NF by requiring every nontrivial determinant to be a superkey; it is not the first stage described.

Question 175

Study-guide references

FDB02 · Data models and product names

Correct answer: D — Network model.

FDB02 specifically describes the classic network model as records connected by pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. That is the representation in the stem. Hierarchical data is restricted to a parent-child tree; relational data uses tables; object-oriented data emphasizes identity, state and behavior. Pointer-linked records therefore identify the network model in this comparison.

Evidence in the complete study note

A data model supplies concepts for structure, relationships, constraints and operations. Conceptual ER models describe entities and relationships. Relational models use tables; hierarchical models organize records in a parent–child tree; classic network models connect records through pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. Object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. A physical model specifies record layouts and access paths. These are different representation choices, not stages of one query.FDB02

D · correct — Classic network databases connect records through pointer-based owner/member links.

Question 178

What is the purpose of a database schema?

Distinguish a relation's structure from its current contents and its descriptive metadata.

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB03 · Relations, schemas, instances and metadata

Correct answer: C — To define the structure and organization of a database

A schema is the database description specified during design. It expresses structure, types, relationships and constraints; enforcing a particular constraint is narrower than the description's overall purpose.

Evidence in the complete study note

A schema describes names, structures and constraints; an instance, state or snapshot is the data at a particular time. Inserting a student changes the state, while adding a column changes the schema. Metadata is data describing data. The catalog or data dictionary records table definitions, types, constraints and often access privileges and optimizer statistics. A schema diagram draws tables and key references; labels such as database diagram can overlap.FDB03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Types are included, but enforcement alone is narrower than the schema description. (PDF pp. 7, 7.)

B · incorrect — Files hold data physically; the schema describes the database. (PDF pp. 7, 7.)

C · correct — Structure and organization match the schema definition. (PDF pp. 7, 7.)

D · incorrect — Integrity conditions can be specified in a schema, but integrity alone is not its overall descriptive purpose. (PDF pp. 7, 7.)

Question 180

Which database model represents data as a hierarchical structure with parent-child relationships?

  • A. Hierarchical model
  • B. Relational model
  • C. Object-oriented model
  • D. Network model

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 149

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Study-guide sections

FDB02 · Data models and product names

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB02 · Data models and product names

Correct answer: A — Hierarchical model.

The defining phrase is a hierarchical parent-child structure. FDB02 assigns that tree organization to the hierarchical model. The relational model uses tables; the object model represents identity, state and behavior; the network model permits more general links including multiple parents. A parent-child tree therefore selects A directly.

Evidence in the complete study note

A data model supplies concepts for structure, relationships, constraints and operations. Conceptual ER models describe entities and relationships. Relational models use tables; hierarchical models organize records in a parent–child tree; classic network models connect records through pointer-based owner/member links, allowing multiple parents. Object models represent objects with identity, state and behavior. A physical model specifies record layouts and access paths. These are different representation choices, not stages of one query.FDB02

Choice by choice

B · incorrect — The relational model represents data as tables rather than a prescribed parent-child tree.

C · incorrect — The object model represents object identity, state and behavior rather than requiring a tree.

D · incorrect — The network model permits more general connections and multiple parents rather than the restricted tree structure.

Question 181

What is the first step in the database design process?

  • A. Defining relationships between tables
  • B. Determining the database schema
  • C. Analyzing the information requirements
  • D. Creating database queries

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 150

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the design phase from its inputs and intended deliverable.

Study-guide sections

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

Correct answer: C — Analyzing the information requirements

The initial design step is to understand the organization's information needs, required transactions, reports and constraints. Those requirements guide conceptual modeling, which is then mapped into a logical schema. Defining table relationships and determining the schema therefore depend on the earlier analysis. Queries express required operations against a model rather than replacing the initial work of identifying what information and behavior are needed. C is the first step in that sequence.

Evidence in the complete study note

First collect business facts, required transactions, reports, volumes, privacy rules and quality targets. Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model. Logical design maps the conceptual model to relations, keys and constraints and checks normalization. Physical design chooses file organization, indexes and storage. Implementation creates and loads the actual objects; testing checks requirements.FDB05

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: table relationships are designed to satisfy requirements already identified.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: schema choices follow the analysis of required information and operations.

C · correct — Correct: analyze information requirements before choosing the model and schema.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: query construction depends on understanding the required information and intended database structure.

Question 182

Read pairs left→right. Student↔Course and Product↔Supplier permit many occurrences in both directions. Each employee belongs to one department with many employees. Each order belongs to one customer, who may place many orders. Which pair is one-to-many?

  • A. Student and Course
  • B. Employee and Department
  • C. Customer and Order
  • D. Product and Supplier

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 151

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Read the relationship in both directions and check participation constraints.

Study-guide sections

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

Correct answer: C — C. Customer and Order

Customer→Order is one-to-many because one customer can place several orders and each order belongs to one customer. Employee→Department runs in the opposite direction: many employees share one department, making it many-to-one. The student/course and product/supplier pairs explicitly permit many partners in both directions and are many-to-many. Cardinality follows these rules and the direction of the pair.

Evidence in the complete study note

Cardinality specifies maximum associated occurrences: 1:1, 1:N or M:N. Minimum cardinality specifies optional/partial participation (zero permitted) or mandatory/total participation (at least one). A maximum of one does not mean participation is compulsory. Relationship degree counts participating entity roles: unary recursive, binary or ternary.FDB09
If each employee belongs to one department and each department has many employees, Employee→Department is N:1; Department→Employee is 1:N.FDB09
Read business rules in both directions.FDB09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Student→Course is M:N under the stated enrollment rule.

B · incorrect — Employee→Department is N:1.

C · correct — Customer→Order is 1:N under the ownership rule.

D · incorrect — Product→Supplier is M:N under the stated supply rule.

Question 183

Which of the following is a benefit of using database indexes?

  • A. Increased data redundancy
  • B. Decreased data integrity
  • C. Improved data retrieval performance
  • D. Simplified data modeling

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare index organization with its access benefits and maintenance costs.

Study-guide sections

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Correct answer: C — Improved data retrieval performance

The auxiliary index provides alternative access paths to speed retrieval under search conditions. This is the directly supported benefit.

Evidence in the complete study note

An index stores search keys with pointers or row identifiers. It often speeds selective retrieval but consumes space and must be maintained on writes. A full scan can win for a tiny table or a query returning most rows. Indexes do not themselves establish referential integrity.FDB25
Secondary indexes provide other access paths and must represent all searchable values with record pointers or pointer lists.FDB25
A dense index represents each search value; sparse indexes rely on ordered data between entries.FDB25

Choice by choice

B · incorrect — Reduced integrity is not a stated benefit. (PDF pp. 21.)

C · correct — Retrieval improvement is explicitly stated. (PDF pp. 21.)

D · incorrect — Simplification of the logical model is not the index's stated function. (PDF pp. 21.)

Question 184

Employee has required, unique SSN and Employee ID values. Names and salaries may repeat. Which listed individual attributes are candidate keys? Select all that apply.

  • A. Social Security Number (SSN)
  • B. Employee Name
  • C. Employee ID
  • D. Employee Salary

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 155

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Test uniqueness and minimality separately when classifying keys.

Study-guide sections

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answers (select all): A, C — A. Social Security Number (SSN); C. Employee ID

A candidate key is a minimal attribute set guaranteed to identify a tuple. Each required unique single-attribute identifier is already minimal, so SSN and Employee ID both qualify. Names and salaries may repeat and therefore cannot guarantee identification. The question asks for candidate keys rather than the one candidate later selected as the primary key, allowing both identifiers.

Evidence in the complete study note

A superkey uniquely identifies a tuple. A candidate key is a minimal superkey: removing any component loses uniqueness. Minimal means no unnecessary attribute, not necessarily the fewest columns among all keys. A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys. A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07
If ID is unique, both {ID} and {ID,City} are superkeys, but only {ID} is minimal.FDB07
Prefer stable, compact identifiers when practical, but Email, SSN or EmployeeID is a candidate only if business rules guarantee uniqueness.FDB07

Choice by choice

A · correct — Required unique SSN identifies each employee and is minimal.

B · incorrect — Repeated names are permitted, so Name is not a candidate key.

C · correct — Required unique Employee ID independently identifies each employee.

D · incorrect — Repeated salaries are permitted, so Salary is not a candidate key.

Question 185

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

Correct answer: C — To establish relationships between tables

A foreign key links a child occurrence to the matching parent candidate/primary key. Its defining purpose is the intertable relationship.

Evidence in the complete study note

Domain integrity restricts type and allowed values. Key integrity enforces uniqueness. Entity integrity requires every primary-key component to be non-NULL. Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed. A parent need not have a child.FDB15
A primary key is one selected candidate key; remaining candidates are alternate keys.FDB07
A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Correct answer: D — Data abstraction

Abstraction hides irrelevant details and helps achieve data independence. Three-schema separation then permits changes at one level without forcing higher-level schema changes.

Evidence in the complete study note

Physical data independence permits changing internal storage without changing the conceptual schema, for example adding an index. Question 187

Which type of database relationship is represented by a join table?

  • A. One-to-one relationship
  • B. One-to-many relationship
  • C. Many-to-many relationship
  • D. Hierarchical relationship

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 158

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify relationship degree and cardinality before choosing a relational mapping.

Study-guide sections

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

FDB11 · Map 1:1 and 1:N relationships

Correct answer: C — Many-to-many relationship.

A join table stores pairs of references to the two participating entity tables, optionally with relationship attributes. FDB12 uses Enrollment(StudentID,CourseID,Grade) for the many-to-many Student–Course relationship. One-to-one and one-to-many relationships normally use a foreign key in an entity table instead. A hierarchy is a structural pattern, not the relationship cardinality represented by this usual association mapping.

Evidence in the complete study note

A binary M:N relationship needs an association table in addition to the two entity tables. Enrollment(StudentID,CourseID,Grade) references Student and Course; (StudentID,CourseID) is a key if one enrollment per pair is allowed. Repeated attempts require Term or AttemptNo in the key. This ordinarily produces three tables.FDB12
For 1:N, place the one-side key as a foreign key in the many-side table; store relationship attributes there.FDB11
For 1:1, one side may hold the other key with a UNIQUE constraint.FDB11

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A 1:1 relationship normally uses a UNIQUE foreign key rather than requiring a join table.

B · incorrect — A 1:N relationship normally stores the one-side key in the many-side entity table.

C · correct — A join or association table stores the pairs making up a many-to-many relationship.

D · incorrect — Hierarchical organization is not the standard cardinality category represented by this association-table mapping.

Question 188

Employee ID uniquely identifies an employee and their name, salary and department. Names may repeat; the same salary may occur in different departments, and a department may contain different salaries. Which listed functional dependency is guaranteed?

  • A. Employee ID -> Employee Name
  • B. Employee Name -> Employee ID
  • C. Employee Salary -> Employee Department
  • D. Employee Department -> Employee Salary

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 159

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute closures using the declared dependencies and test minimality.

Study-guide sections

FDB17 · Functional dependencies and attribute closure

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB17 · Functional dependencies and attribute closure

Correct answer: A — A. Employee ID -> Employee Name

A functional dependency is guaranteed by business rules for every legal state. Unique Employee ID determines all employee attributes, including Name, so A follows. Repeated names can correspond to different IDs, equal salaries can occur in different departments, and one department may contain different salaries. Those permissions prevent the other proposed directions from being guaranteed dependencies.

Evidence in the complete study note

X→Y means every legal pair of tuples agreeing on X also agrees on Y. It is a business constraint, not a correlation observed in one sample. Full dependency requires all determinant attributes; partial dependency holds through a proper subset. A prime attribute belongs to some candidate key; a nonprime attribute belongs to none.FDB17
Remove attributes and recompute to test minimality.FDB17

Choice by choice

A · correct — The unique employee identifier determines the employee's name.

B · incorrect — Repeated names can belong to different IDs.

C · incorrect — The same salary may occur in different departments.

D · incorrect — One department may contain employees with different salaries.

Question 190

Compared with scanning all pages of a large heap file, which advantage can an RDBMS with a suitable index provide for a query selecting a small fraction of rows? Assume index lookup costs less than the scan.

  • A. Easy integration with external systems
  • B. Lower storage requirements
  • C. No need for data normalization
  • D. Faster data retrieval

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 161

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare design techniques by the specific problem each solves.

Study-guide sections

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB01 · Database approach, benefits and costs

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

Correct answer: D — D. Faster data retrieval

The suitable index provides a search path for the selective query, reducing the work compared with scanning the large heap under the stated cost assumption. This supports faster retrieval for this workload. It does not remove normalization concerns, guarantee smaller storage or establish external-system compatibility. Index space and maintenance costs remain separate trade-offs from the query's retrieval advantage.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normalization reduces dependency-driven anomalies. Indexing supplies search paths. Denormalization deliberately repeats or precomputes facts to reduce selected read costs, increasing maintenance work. Partitioning divides rows or columns; replication creates copies to improve locality or availability while complicating updates. These techniques can coexist. An e-commerce or gaming label alone does not select one.FDB38
It often speeds selective retrieval but consumes space and must be maintained on writes.FDB25
A full scan can win for a tiny table or a query returning most rows.FDB25

B · incorrect — Indexes consume additional storage rather than guaranteeing a reduction.

C · incorrect — An index does not remove the need for dependency-aware design.

D · correct — The stated cheaper indexed lookup supports faster retrieval for this query.

Question 193

Which normal forms have defining restrictions on functional dependencies? Select all that apply. Treat 1NF as the atomic-value/no-repeating-groups requirement.

  • A. First Normal Form (1NF)
  • B. Second Normal Form (2NF)
  • C. Third Normal Form (3NF)
  • D. Boyce-Codd Normal Form (BCNF)

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 165

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Test determinants and prime attributes against the stated normal-form rules.

Study-guide sections

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

Correct answers (select all): B, C, D — B. Second Normal Form (2NF); C. Third Normal Form (3NF); D. Boyce-Codd Normal Form (BCNF)

Second normal form excludes nonprime partial functional dependencies on candidate keys. Third normal form checks each nontrivial dependency using the determinant's superkey status or the dependent's prime status. BCNF strengthens that test by requiring a superkey determinant. These three therefore use functional-dependency restrictions. First normal form instead establishes the atomic-value foundation and absence of repeating groups.

Evidence in the complete study note

For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey. The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap. Remove dependencies of nonprime facts on nonkey determinants.FDB21
A relation whose every candidate key is single-attribute has no nonprime partial-key dependency.FDB20
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — 1NF addresses atomic values and repeating groups rather than the stated dependency-based tests.

B · correct — 2NF is defined through absence of nonprime partial functional dependencies on candidate keys.

C · correct — 3NF tests nontrivial FDs using determinant superkeys and prime dependent attributes.

D · correct — BCNF requires every nontrivial FD determinant to be a superkey.

Question 195

Which option states a measurable nonfunctional quality requirement for a database system?

  • A. The database service must achieve at least 99.9% uptime.
  • B. User authentication
  • C. Entity-Relationship (ER) diagram
  • D. Data normalization

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 167

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare design techniques by the specific problem each solves.

Study-guide sections

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB05 · Requirements through implementation

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: A — A. The database service must achieve at least 99.9% uptime.

A nonfunctional requirement states a quality target such as availability or response time. The uptime threshold specifies a measurable availability requirement and fits that category. User authentication names system behavior, while an ER diagram is a modeling artifact and normalization is a design technique. These may support requirements, but they do not themselves state the quantified quality requested here.

Evidence in the complete study note

Privacy requires a threat model. Authentication verifies identity; role-based authorization limits allowed records/actions; encryption protects data at rest or in transit; auditing records activity. Both encryption and access control can protect patient data. Scalability, availability, fast retrieval, redundancy control and security are all reasonable design considerations. Functional requirements describe behavior; nonfunctional requirements quantify qualities such as 99.9% uptime or response time. Authentication and encryption mechanisms alone do not uniquely establish that distinction.FDB38
Adding a generated row ID may make insertion technically possible, but does not remove repeated facts or fix the dependency design.FDB19
Conceptual design turns them into an ER/EER model.FDB05

A · correct — The 99.9% uptime threshold is a measurable availability target.

B · incorrect — Authentication names behavior rather than a quantified quality target.

C · incorrect — An ER diagram is a design artifact.

D · incorrect — Normalization is a design technique.

Question 196

What is the purpose of data manipulation in database management?

  • A. To define the database schema
  • B. To create database backups
  • C. To insert, update, and delete data
  • D. To manage concurrent access to data

Source

Study-guide sections

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB26 · SQL command categories and exact scope

Correct answer: C — To insert, update, and delete data

The DML subsection includes INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE. SQL examples show insertion/deletion and an UPDATE privilege permits changing values, grounding the data-manipulation purpose. DML also includes retrieval, so C describes its modification part rather than all of DML.

Evidence in the complete study note

DDL changes structure: CREATE, ALTER, DROP, commonly RENAME and TRUNCATE in textbook classifications. DML retrieves or changes data: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Some texts separate SELECT as DQL. DCL manages privileges through GRANT and REVOKE. TCL includes COMMIT, ROLLBACK and SAVEPOINT. Classify a statement by its effect, not its length.FDB26

D · incorrect — Concurrency control is a separate transaction-management responsibility. (PDF pp. 26, 55, 77.)

Question 198

Which are legitimate considerations when designing a database for a large e-commerce site? Select all that apply. Minimal redundancy means controlling avoidable duplication while permitting deliberate replicas.

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

Correct answers (select all): A, B, C, D — A. High availability and scalability; B. Minimal data redundancy; C. Fast data retrieval; D. Secure user authentication

Large e-commerce databases need several complementary qualities. Availability and scalability support continued service and growth; retrieval speed supports interactive use; security protects accounts and records. Controlling avoidable redundancy reduces consistency and maintenance problems even where intentional replicas are useful. C · correct — Fast retrieval is a legitimate quality target for browsing, orders and customer-facing operations.

D · correct — Secure authentication is a legitimate security consideration for customer and administrative access.

Question 200

Which techniques can improve query performance for some suitable workloads, without implying that they always help? Select all that apply.

  • A. Indexing
  • B. Data replication
  • C. Denormalization
  • D. Partitioning

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 173

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare design techniques by the specific problem each solves.

Study-guide sections

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

FDB24 · Records, pages and primary file organization

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB25 · Ordered, dense, sparse and multilevel indexes

FDB24 · Records, pages and primary file organization

Correct answers (select all): A, B, C, D — A. Indexing; B. Data replication; C. Denormalization; D. Partitioning

Indexes add search paths for selective queries. Replicas can improve read locality, denormalization can reduce selected joins or computations, and partitioning can reduce relevant data access when aligned with queries. Each technique can therefore help some workloads. Their benefits are conditional and can involve extra storage or update costs, so selecting all four does not mean every database should use all of them.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normalization reduces dependency-driven anomalies. Indexing supplies search paths. Denormalization deliberately repeats or precomputes facts to reduce selected read costs, increasing maintenance work. Partitioning divides rows or columns; replication creates copies to improve locality or availability while complicating updates. These techniques can coexist. An e-commerce or gaming label alone does not select one.FDB38
B/B+ trees support ordered and range search; hashing targets equality; bitmap indexes suit some analytic workloads.FDB25
Choose organization from actual reads, writes and predicates.FDB24

Choice by choice

A · correct — Indexes can reduce reads for selective predicates when maintenance and lookup costs are justified.

B · correct — Replication can place read copies near users or spread suitable read workloads.

C · correct — Denormalization can avoid selected joins or repeated computation by storing redundant/precomputed facts.

D · correct — Partitioning can limit relevant data or improve locality when partitioning matches query predicates.

Question 201

Users may follow many other users, and each user may be followed by many users. Which option describes the cardinality of this recursive user relationship, rather than a key constraint or procedural enforcement mechanism?

  • A. Primary key constraints
  • B. Foreign key constraints
  • C. Many-to-many relationships
  • D. Triggers and stored procedures

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 174

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify relationship degree and cardinality before choosing a relational mapping.

Study-guide sections

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB12 · Map M:N, recursive and n-ary relationships

FDB09 · Cardinality, participation and direction

FDB07 · Superkeys, candidate keys and primary keys

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answer: C — C. Many-to-many relationships

The follow rules permit multiple related users in both directions, which is many-to-many cardinality. A recursive association table can store two user references to represent those links. Primary and foreign keys support identification and referential integrity, but they are constraint mechanisms rather than the requested multiplicity classification. Triggers and stored procedures are also not a cardinality category.

Evidence in the complete study note

A recursive M:N friendship uses UserLink(UserID,OtherUserID), two foreign keys to User; decide whether links are directed and whether self-links are allowed. A recursive 1:N supervision uses a nullable SupervisorID reference in Employee. For a ternary Supply(SupplierID,PartID,ProjectID,Quantity), preserve all participating references; binary replacements can lose which triple occurred. Choose the association key from actual cardinalities rather than automatically assuming every reference is necessary.FDB12
A composite key contains multiple attributes and need not be the chosen primary key.FDB07
Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Primary keys identify rows rather than name relationship multiplicity.

B · incorrect — Foreign keys validate references rather than describe the many-to-many cardinality.

C · correct — Both endpoints permit many partners, establishing M:N.

D · incorrect — Procedures are implementation mechanisms rather than a cardinality type.

Question 202

In the usual normalization sequence, which is the first normal form excluding transitive dependence of nonprime attributes on a key through a nonkey determinant?

  • A. First Normal Form (1NF)
  • B. Second Normal Form (2NF)
  • C. Third Normal Form (3NF)
  • D. Boyce-Codd Normal Form (BCNF)

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 175

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check atomicity, candidate keys and partial dependencies in that order.

Study-guide sections

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB20 · First and second normal forms

FDB21 · Third normal form, BCNF and higher forms

Correct answer: C — Third Normal Form (3NF)

In the stated single-key teaching case, 2NF excludes partial dependencies but can still allow a nonprime fact to depend on the key through a nonkey determinant. The next defining stage is 3NF, summarized by the whole-key-and-nothing-but-the-key mnemonic. BCNF strengthens the determinant rule, and 4NF addresses multivalued dependencies. Stronger forms inherit earlier conditions, so the explicit first-stage scope makes 3NF the unique selection rather than treating inherited properties as rival answers.

Evidence in the complete study note

1NF requires atomic values in the chosen relational domains and no repeating groups in cells.FDB20
2NF requires 1NF and no nonprime attribute partially dependent on any candidate key.FDB20
For every nontrivial FD X→A, 3NF permits it when X is a superkey or A is prime. BCNF requires X to be a superkey. The mnemonic whole key and nothing but the key describes the common single-key case; it cannot replace the full tests when keys overlap. Remove dependencies of nonprime facts on nonkey determinants.FDB21

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Incorrect: 1NF concerns atomic values and does not eliminate the specified transitive dependencies.

B · incorrect — Incorrect: 2NF excludes partial dependencies but can retain a nonprime transitive dependency through a nonkey determinant.

C · correct — Correct: 3NF is the first stage imposing the specified nonprime transitive-dependency restriction in this teaching case.

D · incorrect — Incorrect: BCNF strengthens 3NF by requiring every nontrivial determinant to be a superkey; it is not the first stage described.

Question 203

What is the purpose of denormalization in database design for real-world scenarios?

  • A. To eliminate data redundancy
  • B. To optimize query performance
  • C. To enforce referential integrity
  • D. To simplify the database schema

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 176

Compare design techniques by the specific problem each solves.

Study-guide sections

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB38 · Choose a design technique from the requirement

FDB15 · Integrity and constraint taxonomy

Correct answer: B — To improve selected query performance.

Denormalization repeats or precomputes facts so that selected queries can perform less work, such as fewer joins. FDB38 also states the cost: more maintenance when underlying facts change. It does not aim to eliminate redundancy or enforce foreign-key references. A simpler query path may result, but a simpler schema is not the essential purpose.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normalization reduces dependency-driven anomalies. Indexing supplies search paths. Denormalization deliberately repeats or precomputes facts to reduce selected read costs, increasing maintenance work. Partitioning divides rows or columns; replication creates copies to improve locality or availability while complicating updates. These techniques can coexist. An e-commerce or gaming label alone does not select one.FDB38
Referential integrity requires a non-NULL foreign-key value to match an eligible referenced unique/candidate key; self-references are allowed.FDB15
Key integrity enforces uniqueness.FDB15

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Denormalization introduces controlled redundancy instead of eliminating it.

B · correct — Its goal is to reduce costs of selected read queries using repeated or precomputed information.

C · incorrect — Foreign-key constraints enforce references; denormalization itself does not establish referential integrity.

D · incorrect — It may simplify selected reads, but schema simplicity is not the defining or guaranteed goal.

Question 204

Which listed modeling technique directly includes superclass/subclass inheritance? Interpret ER modeling in A as basic ER without the EER extension, relational modeling as ordinary tables/keys, and XML modeling as document structure.

Study-guide references

FDB13 · EER inheritance and category constraints

Correct answer: B — B. Object-oriented data modeling

Object-oriented data modeling directly represents superclass/subclass inheritance. Enhanced ER also supports inheritance, but A is explicitly limited to basic ER in this question. Ordinary relational tables can encode subtype data through references without making inheritance a direct modeling construct, and document structure is not the object inheritance mechanism being tested. The scope distinction makes the selected technique definite.

Evidence in the complete study note

EER extends ER with superclass/subclass structure. A subclass inherits the superclass attributes and relationships. Specialization divides Student into Undergraduate and Graduate; generalization combines common properties into Student. Disjoint subclasses cannot overlap; overlapping subclasses can. Object models also support inheritance; distinguish basic ER notation from EER when comparing models.FDB13
Structural EER inheritance alone does not define executable object methods.FDB13

Question 206

FDB02 · Data models and product names

FDB-S02 · Relational systems with richer types

A hierarchical structure organizes elements into parent-child levels. FDB02 assigns this representation to the hierarchical model, so C directly matches the requested design concept. Evidence in the complete study note

Question 207

Which benefit directly follows from reducing dependency-driven insertion, deletion and update anomalies through normalization, without assuming reduced storage or simpler queries?

  • A. Simplified data retrieval
  • B. Increased data redundancy
  • C. Enhanced data integrity
  • D. Reduced storage requirements

Adapted from

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 181

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.

Study-guide sections

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB19 · Insertion, deletion and update anomalies

Correct answer: C — C. Enhanced data integrity

Normalization separates facts according to their determinants, reducing repeated facts and the insertion, deletion and update anomalies they cause. That directly supports enhanced data integrity. Storage savings and simpler retrieval are not guaranteed because decomposition can introduce additional keys and joins. Increased redundancy is opposite to the intended reduction of avoidable repetition, so it is not the benefit identified by this question.

Evidence in the complete study note

Suppose Enrollment repeats StudentName for every course. An update anomaly requires changing every copy of the student's name; missed copies disagree. An insertion anomaly prevents recording a new student until some enrollment exists. A deletion anomaly loses the only record of a student's details when their last enrollment is deleted. Revoke anomaly is not a normalization anomaly.FDB19
Normalization separates facts according to their determinants, reducing avoidable repetition and associated anomalies.FDB19
Reduced redundancy can improve integrity and storage use, but extra keys and joins mean neither storage savings nor faster retrieval is guaranteed.FDB19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Normalized retrieval may require more joins, so simplification does not follow.

B · incorrect — Normalization aims to reduce avoidable repetition.

C · correct — Reducing inconsistent updates and related anomalies improves integrity.

D · incorrect — Storage savings can be offset by extra keys and relation overhead.

Question 208

Which database design concept allows the division of a large database into smaller, manageable units?

  • A. Data partitioning
  • B. Database replication
  • C. Data migration
  • D. Data archiving

Source

  • Computer Science MCQ practice collection · Question 182

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish logical records, disk pages and physical file organization.

Study-guide sections

FDB24 · Records, pages and primary file organization

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB24 · Records, pages and primary file organization

Correct answer: A — Data partitioning.

FDB24 explicitly contrasts partitioning, replication, migration and archiving. Partitioning divides the dataset into smaller portions, matching the question. Replication makes additional copies, migration moves data, and archiving takes older data out of active service. Those operations can accompany partitioning, but none of them defines division into manageable units.

Evidence in the complete study note

A page/block is a unit of database I/O and holds several records. For fixed unspanned records of size r in usable page space B, the blocking factor is floor(B/r), and n records occupy ceil(n/blocking factor) pages. Page headers and slots reduce usable space.FDB24
Partitioning splits a large dataset; replication copies it; migration moves it; archiving removes older data from active service.FDB24
A heap has no search-key order and supports simple inserts, but searches may scan all pages.FDB24

Choice by choice

A · correct — Partitioning divides a large dataset into smaller portions or manageable units.

B · incorrect — Replication creates copies rather than dividing the original dataset into distinct units.

C · incorrect — Migration moves data to another location or system rather than defining a split.

D · incorrect — Archiving removes older data from active service rather than generally dividing the database.

Question 210

In contrary to the traditional file-based approach, in the database approach:

  • A. Programs define and access their own data
  • B. Store data items in multiple copies
  • C. Don't care about the definition of programs and data
  • D. Data and programs are defined independently

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 49

Trace which schema levels a proposed change affects.

Study-guide sections

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

FDB04 · Three-schema architecture and data independence

Correct answer: D — Data and programs are defined independently

The database approach insulates programs from data definitions and supports data independence. This is the relevant distinction from programs managing their own files.

Evidence in the complete study note

Physical data independence permits changing internal storage without changing the conceptual schema, for example adding an index. Logical data independence permits conceptual changes while preserving affected external views and application interfaces, for example splitting a table while maintaining a compatible view. It is harder because applications depend on logical meaning. Data abstraction hides irrelevant details; program–data insulation separates data definitions from application code.FDB04

C · incorrect — The approach explicitly maintains metadata and definitions, not indifference to them. (PDF pp. 5, 6, 8.)

D · correct — Independent definitions match program/data insulation and independence. (PDF pp. 5, 6, 8.)

What is the purpose of normalization in database design?

Distinguish dependency-based design problems from the contents of one sample table.
